Exact physical suffix products and the selected terminal law\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum of a nonnegative integer L, including\nsbit(0)=0. For 0<=L<=n define the product of the FINAL L actual factors:\n\n    r_L=product_(t=n-L)^(n-1)(h_t-1), r_0=1.\n\nThe index j counted backward from the final physical step has\nh_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(2^m-j)=a*c^v2(j), for 1<=j<2^m. The valuation equality\nfollows by factoring the exact power of2 from j: the remaining difference\nfrom2^m is odd. For any L, sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t).\nWriting L in binary and summing each digit's contributions gives\nsum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t)=L-sbit(L). Therefore the EXACT suffix identity is\n\n    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), 0<=L<=n.                 (3.1)\n\nNo product or recurrence has been numerically evaluated. In particular\ns:=r_n=beta^n/c^m, and the signed full-word product is -s since n is odd.\nDefine the actual terminal coefficient and its absolute scale by\n\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_(i<n)u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIf L=n-1-i, adding the next backward factor shows\n\n    u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^L u_i=(-1)^i u_i. Keep arbitrary independent\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with every original position defined by\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nThe residual orientation is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal actual jets obey Q_ij<=0. For i<n let y_i=x_i-g_i and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, with the corresponding star quantities zero.\nLet e be the all-ones column of length n, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nT=T82 the lower triangular matrix T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i.\nFor vector columns g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1)) and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1)),\n\n    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y).                    (4.1)\n\nThis is invertible on the ENTIRE formal space. Explicitly, if\npi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), then\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (4.2)\n\nSubstitution into x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i proves\n\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.           (4.3)\n\nFor the latter identity, h_j=1-(1-h_j) telescopes on each suffix:\nk_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. In particular e^T v=p+s.\nThe cumulative terminal probabilities, distinct from detail weights below,\nare Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j. Define\n\n    R_i=h_i*Cprob_i-alpha_i,\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t=Te,\n    Uclock=sum_i R_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s).\n\nThen T^T v=R and\nR_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                  (4.7)\n\nSigns are not presumed. This lists every original coefficient, including\nboth optimum orientations and the actual outgoing terminal distribution.\nFor each prefix index i, the outgoing sum is N_ii, while the incoming\nPREFIX sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. The incoming terminal coefficient2alpha_i\ntherefore gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal divergence is2;\noptimum divergence is-2. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.  (5.2)\n\nThe charge J is paid ONCE globally. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15.                (6.7)\n\nThe strict lower bounds use a*b>4/9, gamma>1, R_0>bp, U>7/3 and\nrho_0<1/10. On the other hand Aword>128 gives\n\n    qtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]<8/15,             (6.8)\n\nbecause this inequality is equivalent to Aword>128. Thus no member of\nLaw A has both full PSD and lambda_(0,z)>=0 at q<=qtar. This argument\nuses the exact SINGLE global charge and no local Schur-budget summation.\n\n### 6.2 A stronger literal incoming-optimum failure for every q\n\nThe weighted clock is uniformly large. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. This excludes\nLaw A as an original-row certificate even at larger q. It does not exclude\na balanced re-selection, which can change nu_1 and (6.5). Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H.      (7.4)\n\nIn particular ALL original finite first-row multipliers are nonnegative\niff S0<=0, equivalently\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).           (7.5)\n\nThis realizes the invariant (5.4), rather than assuming it can be repaired.\nIt changes the demanding Law A quantity U-1+p in (6.6) to the much smaller\nH/(1-rho_0). Thus the Law A charge obstruction does NOT transfer to Law B.\n\nThe simultaneous first-row and full-PSD conditions at qtar are nonvacuous.\nWithin this same two-parameter law choose the explicit member\n\n    kappa_0=gamma*R_0*H/[a*u_0*(1-rho_0)],\n    tau_0=2*gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).                    (7.6)\n\nThese are positive. Equation (7.4) gives N^B_0j=0 for every j>0, and\nJ_A=a*gamma*R_0*H. From (2.1), gamma<2 and (4.4),\n\n    J_A< (25p/16)*(9p/8)=225p^2/128<2p^2.\n\nConsequently p^2+J_A<3p^2<p/2<qtar, because p<1/128 and\nqtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]>17/32>1/2. This is a strictly positive\nfull factor at the target with all finite first-row multipliers zero,\npositive terminal probabilities, and positive outgoing optimum multipliers.\nIt remains a FAILED certificate because the complete array still has a\nnegative original coefficient, as the next subsection proves.\n\n### 7.2 Last-to-previous-leaf versus incoming optimum1: exhaustive sign conflict\n\nLet j=z-2, the genuine preceding queried leaf. Both z and j are even.\nFor i,j>0 the balanced correction has the explicit entry\n\n    B_B,ij=xi*(v_i*r_j^pool-r_i^pool*v_j).            (7.7)\n\nBy (3.7), v_z/u_z=v_j/u_j=rplus. Therefore B_B,zj=0. Also\nA_A,zj=0. The last row of T^T is the last coordinate row, so exactly\n\n    N^B_zj=u_z*u_j*(-kappa/U+gamma*rplus^2). Otherwise its sign passes and necessarily\n\n    kappa>=gamma*U*rplus^2>7/12.                     (7.9)\n\nThe strict lower bound follows from gamma>1, U>7/3 and rplus>1/2.\nWe now prove that throughout this remaining domain nu^B_1 is negative.\n\nPut tpool=sum_i r_i^pool*t_i=(tbar-rho_0)/(1-rho_0). Then\nbvec^T t=xi*(Uclock-v_0-H*tpool), chi^T t=1-tpool.\nFor i>0, using (7.3) gives the exact expression\n\n    nu^B_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)\n       +[gamma*Uclock-xi*(tpool-1)]*v_i\n       +xi*r_i^pool*(Uclock-(p+s))-tau*rho_i-2alpha_i. With p<1/128, (7.12) implies\nxi/(U-u_0)<9p/2<1<gamma*rminus. Thus the coefficient of Uclock in\n(7.11) is negative; Uclock>0. The terms -xi*(p+s)/(U-u_0), -tau/U and\n-2delta are nonpositive, with the first and last strictly negative.\nFurther,\n\n    tpool < tbar/(1-rho_0),\n    xi*rminus*tpool < (9p)*(3/2)*(10/9)*tbar\n                                      =15p*tbar.    (7.13)\n\nSince t_1<tbar/2 by (6.9), dropping the negative terms in (7.11) gives\n\n    nu^B_1/u_1<(-kappa/2+15p)*tbar<0.               (7.14)\n\nFor the final strict sign, (7.9) gives kappa/2>7/24, whereas\n15p<15/128<7/24. All denominators and u_1,tbar are positive.\n\nCombining (7.8) and (7.14) covers EVERY kappa>0,tau>=0, with no assumption\non PSD or the first-row signs: either lambda_(z,z-2)<0, or\nlambda_(star,1)=nu^B_1<0. At equality kappa=gamma*U*rplus^2, (7.9) and\n(7.14) still apply. This excludes the WHOLE displayed Law B domain at any\nq, in particular every full-PSD member at qtar, including (7.6).\n\nThis conflict concerns the literal balanced correction (7.1). Another\nbalanced matrix can change the pair (7.8), the optimum row (7.11), or both.\nNo universal exclusion of balanced re-selection is asserted.\n\n## 8. Boundary, sign and scope audit\n\n* All enclosing integers m>=1536 are treated with the SAME beta,c,a at\n  every child. No finite numerical horizon test or reset is used.\n* The probabilities at every original queried target and at the optimum\n  are strictly positive. The actual terminal gradient remains free, with\n  its exact square retained. No earlier remote row is removed.\n* The variance metric controls all n-1 independent detail directions.\n  The independent common direction and its real derivative are retained.\n* Every finite and optimum coefficient has a literal scalar formula.\n  Terminal and outgoing-optimum signs pass for both laws. Incoming-optimum\n  and finite signs fail as proved; no signed row is called a certificate.\n* tau=0 is allowed and covered; kappa>0 is necessary for the specified\n  positive metric. Equality in PSD and in the tail sign has been covered.\n* Balanced B_B e=0 is checked explicitly, and the initial clock contribution\n  is retained in the exact incoming-optimum formula. This is not the old\n  isotropic endpoint correction or the old physical-tree uniform pool.\n* The first-row invariant includes d_0. Its inverse-metric charge is paid\n  once, with an explicit global square-zero direction proving necessity.\n* Formal negative directions are not actual trajectories. Failure of these\n  two coefficient laws gives no lower bound on W and no global no-certificate\n  theorem. Fact931's missing full-function hypothesis remains unproved.\n\nThe row identities and inequalities above have received a textual author\naudit only. They are not verifier-accepted facts, and no downstream argument\nmay treat this report as an accepted building block.\n\n## 9. Retrieval, failure synthesis and handoff\n\nThe query-memory, immediate-conclusions, direct-proving,\nconstruct-counterexamples and identify-key-failures procedures guided this\nbounded joint attempt. The fact search found no accepted version of the\nnew weighted first-row identity or these two row laws. Current guidance\nwas recalled by its targeted query after an empty-string query returned\ncount0. Oversized initial recall output was truncated; the relevant old\njoint report and accepted inputs were reread in bounded portions.\n\nA repair-mode Matlas search for full PSD weighted interpolation certificates\nreturned unrelated PL, stochastic and constant-step statements; none was\nused. Web search located Altschuler--Parrilo, 'Acceleration by Stepsize\nHedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization',\narXiv:2309.16530, https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530, already read in the prior\nround as exact proof-pattern background. No external theorem or proof step\nis imported here. The new laws reduce to explicit coefficient identities\nin that already-audited interpolation regime; no additional literature\nlayer or new mathematical machinery is needed for these scoped algebraic\nrejections. No notebook or numerical certificate was run.\n\nLaw A's failure is not caused by forgetting tail scales: it uses them in\nboth the metric and terminal distribution, and its imbalance is optimally\ndistributed for its fixed initial component. Its literal first-to-last row\nstill overcharges, and the early negative-k incoming optimum row fails.\n\nLaw B demonstrates a real escape from Law A's first-row charge argument:\nan explicit balanced transport repairs the entire row at affordable full\nPSD cost. Its remaining failure is a different, complete-original-array\nconflict: a same-sign pair of late queried leaves fixes a nonvanishing\nmetric scale, while the early odd query sits below the weighted mean clock\nand has negative terminal coefficient. The specified transport is too\nsmall to pay that original optimum row at that forced metric scale.\n\nThe next missing mechanism would have to alter this second conflict, for\nexample through a different balanced transport affecting the relevant\nlate pair and optimum load, a different genuinely nonisotropic metric, or\na different actual terminal law. These are uncovered freedoms, not a third\nlaw attempted in this invocation and not a solution by an unknown matrix.\nThe first-row invariant (5.4) and its charge (5.5) remain necessary checks\nin the stated general architecture; their sufficiency has not been claimed.\n\nThe two-law allocation is consumed. All those\ncounts remain zero. All scalar inequalities below are textual mathematics.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe exact suffix-product scale is retained in BOTH the nonisotropic detail\nmetric and the actual terminal distribution. Every original queried gradient,\nthe actual unqueried terminal gradient, and the initial vector remain\nindependent. The two explicit laws use the same weighted full square and\ndistributed imbalance, but genuinely different original coefficient arrays.\n\nLaw A uses the canonical weighted skew for that imbalance. Throughout its\nentire domain, its incoming optimum multiplier at original query1 is\nnegative. Independently, making its first-to-last queried multiplier\nnonnegative forces its exact global square charge above the assigned\nqtar=17/(16D). This quantifies why paying through an exponentially tiny\ninitial tail weight fails in this particular matching.\n\nLaw B adds an EXPLICIT BALANCED skew transport, with no sign-feasibility\noracle. It equalizes the entire first row, and can make all its finite\nmultipliers nonnegative while maintaining the full PSD factor at qtar.\nNevertheless, for EVERY member of Law B, either the original multiplier\nfrom the last queried leaf to the preceding queried leaf is negative, or\nthe original incoming optimum multiplier at query1 is negative. This is\nan exhaustive obstruction for this displayed balanced correction, including\narbitrary positive metric scale and arbitrary nonnegative imbalance scale.\n\nNeither exclusion is an impossibility theorem for all balanced corrections,\nall weighted variances, all terminal probabilities, or the full dual cone.\nNo primal interpolability or lower bound on W is inferred from a formal\nnegative direction or a negative signed multiplier. No full upper bound on\nW, benchmark improvement, or resolution of the original problem is obtained.\n\n## 2. Original problem and accepted inputs\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, W_n(h) denotes\nthe supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth function with an\nattained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the original recursion\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The original benchmark retains EVERY integer split:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation, and exact\nincoming-terminal-free reduction. It deletes NO queried-prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-enclosing-m word below,\nits mass identity and the scalar comparison B_n/D<47/50. Its implication\nfrom W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) to a strict benchmark improvement is CONDITIONAL;\nthat full-class upper hypothesis is still missing. These are the only\naccepted mathematical inputs used here. Historical draft6e66efffac0795f1\nis recall only; its coordinate and row identities are rederived below.\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536. All children use this SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, z=n-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, Aword=1+S, D=1+2S=2Aword-1,\n    p=1/Aword, qtar=17/(16D), b=1+a.\n\nThe symbols p and q in this report mean p83 and q83; neither is a benchmark\nexponent or a primal derivative. The letter B without a subscript below is\na skew matrix, not the benchmark B_n. All other hierarchy notation is local.\nThe actual physical step is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2(j) is the exponent\nof2 dividing the positive integer j. The final three steps are\n(b,1+beta,b). The first and last queried steps equal b; index n is the\nACTUAL terminal, distinct from z.\n\nThe elementary bounds needed below are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.       (2.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<17/12; hence ell>12/5 and\nell<29/12, and (29/12)(1+1/6144)<5/2. Also beta>7/8,\na>(7/8)/(5/2)>1/3, and a<1/ell<1/2.\nThe accepted mass formula is\n\n    Aword=(1-vword)2^m+vword*c^m,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).\n\nIndeed c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1 give c(c-2)>1. Consequently\n\n    2^m<Aword<c^m, 0<p<2^(-m)<1/128.                  (2.2)\n\nEvery h_i>1, consistently with these strict mass inequalities.\n\n## 3. Exact physical suffix products and the selected terminal law\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum of a nonnegative integer L, including\nsbit(0)=0. For 0<=L<=n define the product of the FINAL L actual factors:\n\n    r_L=product_(t=n-L)^(n-1)(h_t-1), r_0=1.\n\nThe index j counted backward from the final physical step has\nh_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(2^m-j)=a*c^v2(j), for 1<=j<2^m. The valuation equality\nfollows by factoring the exact power of2 from j: the remaining difference\nfrom2^m is odd. For any L, sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t).\nWriting L in binary and summing each digit's contributions gives\nsum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t)=L-sbit(L). Therefore the EXACT suffix identity is\n\n    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), 0<=L<=n.                 (3.1)\n\nNo product or recurrence has been numerically evaluated. In particular\ns:=r_n=beta^n/c^m, and the signed full-word product is -s since n is odd.\nDefine the actual terminal coefficient and its absolute scale by\n\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_(i<n)u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIf L=n-1-i, adding the next backward factor shows\n\n    u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^L u_i=(-1)^i u_i.       (3.2)\n\nThus all u_i are strictly positive. The last three are\n\n    u_z=b, u_(z-1)=a(1+beta), u_(z-2)=b*a*beta,\n    u_0=(b/a)s.                                      (3.3)\n\nExpanding a product over the m binary digits gives the finite identity\n\n    Z=sum_(L=0)^n r_L=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Z-1-s <2(3/2)^m.                              (3.4)\n\nThe last inequality uses beta<1 and c>2. The three distinct last terms\nin (3.3), with (2.1), give\n\n    U>4/3+5/8+7/18=169/72>7/3.                       (3.5)\n\nBoth laws use these SAME explicit terminal probabilities:\n\n    alpha_star=p, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i=delta*u_i,\n    delta=(1-p)/U, rplus=1-delta, rminus=1+delta,\n    v=k-alpha.                                       (3.6)\n\nThey sum to1 and are strictly positive on every original queried sample\nand the optimum. They are probabilities on real original rows, not on a\nselected child interface. Since U>7/3, one has 0<delta<3/7, rplus>1/2,\nand 1<rminus<3/2. Precisely\n\n    v_i=rplus*u_i for i even,\n    v_i=-rminus*u_i for i odd.                        (3.7)\n\nSeveral uniform small-initial-weight bounds will be used. The binomial\nexpansion implies n=2^m-1>m(m-1)/2>128m for m>=1536. Also\n(1-eps)^(-n)>(1+eps)^n>=1+n*eps, so beta^n<1/33. From Aword<c^m,\ns/p=beta^n*Aword/c^m<beta^n. Because b/a=1+1/a<4,\n\n    s<p/33, u_0<4p/33<p/8,\n    alpha_0<p/8, rho_0<3p/56<1/10.                  (3.8)\n\nThese are symbolic inequalities uniform in all enclosing m in the task.\n\n## 4. Full original coordinates and the complete signed matching\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep arbitrary independent\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with every original position defined by\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nThe residual orientation is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal actual jets obey Q_ij<=0. For i<n let y_i=x_i-g_i and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, with the corresponding star quantities zero.\nLet e be the all-ones column of length n, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nT=T82 the lower triangular matrix T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i.\nFor vector columns g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1)) and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1)),\n\n    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y).                    (4.1)\n\nThis is invertible on the ENTIRE formal space. Explicitly, if\npi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), then\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (4.2)\n\nSubstitution into x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i proves\n\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.           (4.3)\n\nFor the latter identity, h_j=1-(1-h_j) telescopes on each suffix:\nk_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. In particular e^T v=p+s.\nThe cumulative terminal probabilities, distinct from detail weights below,\nare Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j. Define\n\n    R_i=h_i*Cprob_i-alpha_i,\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t=Te,\n    Uclock=sum_i R_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s).\n\nThen T^T v=R and\nR_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                  (4.7)\n\nSigns are not presumed. This lists every original coefficient, including\nboth optimum orientations and the actual outgoing terminal distribution.\nFor each prefix index i, the outgoing sum is N_ii, while the incoming\nPREFIX sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. The incoming terminal coefficient2alpha_i\ntherefore gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal divergence is2;\noptimum divergence is-2. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.  (5.2)\n\nThe charge J is paid ONCE globally. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15.                (6.7)\n\nThe strict lower bounds use a*b>4/9, gamma>1, R_0>bp, U>7/3 and\nrho_0<1/10. On the other hand Aword>128 gives\n\n    qtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]<8/15,             (6.8)\n\nbecause this inequality is equivalent to Aword>128. Thus no member of\nLaw A has both full PSD and lambda_(0,z)>=0 at q<=qtar. This argument\nuses the exact SINGLE global charge and no local Schur-budget summation.\n\n### 6.2 A stronger literal incoming-optimum failure for every q\n\nThe weighted clock is uniformly large. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. This excludes\nLaw A as an original-row certificate even at larger q. It does not exclude\na balanced re-selection, which can change nu_1 and (6.5). Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H.      (7.4)\n\nIn particular ALL original finite first-row multipliers are nonnegative\niff S0<=0, equivalently\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).           (7.5)\n\nThis realizes the invariant (5.4), rather than assuming it can be repaired.\nIt changes the demanding Law A quantity U-1+p in (6.6) to the much smaller\nH/(1-rho_0). Thus the Law A charge obstruction does NOT transfer to Law B.\n\nThe simultaneous first-row and full-PSD conditions at qtar are nonvacuous.\nWithin this same two-parameter law choose the explicit member\n\n    kappa_0=gamma*R_0*H/[a*u_0*(1-rho_0)],\n    tau_0=2*gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).                    (7.6)\n\nThese are positive. Equation (7.4) gives N^B_0j=0 for every j>0, and\nJ_A=a*gamma*R_0*H. From (2.1), gamma<2 and (4.4),\n\n    J_A< (25p/16)*(9p/8)=225p^2/128<2p^2.\n\nConsequently p^2+J_A<3p^2<p/2<qtar, because p<1/128 and\nqtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]>17/32>1/2. This is a strictly positive\nfull factor at the target with all finite first-row multipliers zero,\npositive terminal probabilities, and positive outgoing optimum multipliers.\nIt remains a FAILED certificate because the complete array still has a\nnegative original coefficient, as the next subsection proves.\n\n### 7.2 Last-to-previous-leaf versus incoming optimum1: exhaustive sign conflict\n\nLet j=z-2, the genuine preceding queried leaf. Both z and j are even.\nFor i,j>0 the balanced correction has the explicit entry\n\n    B_B,ij=xi*(v_i*r_j^pool-r_i^pool*v_j).            (7.7)\n\nBy (3.7), v_z/u_z=v_j/u_j=rplus. Therefore B_B,zj=0. The terms -xi*(p+s)/(U-u_0), -tau/U and\n-2delta are nonpositive, with the first and last strictly negative.\nFurther,\n\n    tpool < tbar/(1-rho_0),\n    xi*rminus*tpool < (9p)*(3/2)*(10/9)*tbar\n                                      =15p*tbar.    (7.13)\n\nSince t_1<tbar/2 by (6.9), dropping the negative terms in (7.11) gives\n\n    nu^B_1/u_1<(-kappa/2+15p)*tbar<0.               (7.14)\n\nFor the final strict sign, (7.9) gives kappa/2>7/24, whereas\n15p<15/128<7/24. Same physical word, actual boundary indices, and normalization\n\nFix any integer m>=1536, and throughout set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, Z=D_m=1+2S.\n\nAll children keep this SAME enclosing m and all its parameters. Index\nthe actual steps from0 to n-1. Put\n\n    K=n-2, R=n-1,\n    A=h_0=h_R=1+a, d=a, e=h_K-1=a*c=beta_h,\n    I={1,...,K}, M=sum_(i=1)^(K-1) h_i,\n    B=sum_(i in I)h_i=M+1+e=S-2A,\n    v=M+1=B-e, U=1+d+v,\n    w=A+1/2-A^2/2=1-a^2/2,\n    D=U+e+w=S+1/2-A^2/2.\n\nThus 2D=Z-A^2. Since\nlambda<5/2 and epsilon_h<=1/6144, in fact c<5/2; for example use\nsqrt(2)<10/7, lambda<17/7, and (17/7)(6145/6144)<5/2.\nAlso beta_h>99/100, so\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2, 0<e<1, 7/8<w<1.             (1.2)\n\nThe upper bound on a follows from a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.\nFor the lower bound use a>(99/100)/(5/2)=99/250>1/3.\nThere are n-3 ordinary middle entries, all exceeding1, so v>n-2>5.\nIn particular U>6 and D>18. Much weaker horizon bounds would suffice.\n\nFor later mass comparisons put X_h,j=1+sum H_j. Literal summation gives\nX_h,(j+1)=2X_h,j+a*c^j, X_h,0=1, hence\n\n    X_h,j=(1-v_h)2^j+v_h*c^j, v_h=a/(c-2). A concrete three-sector row identity, rederived jointly\n\nStart by imposing g_i=q for i in I, and put p=g_0, r=g_R. Define\n\n    alpha=A/(2D), theta=(U+e)/(2D-U),\n    beta=theta-alpha, gamma=1-theta,\n    ell=alpha, eta=alpha/(d+v), R_flow=alpha-ell=0,\n    kappa=beta+ell+eta=theta+eta,\n    gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r.                    (2.1)\n\nUse these core weights mu on ORIGINAL rows:\n\n| Row | mu |\n|---|---:|\n| Q_(0,star) | alpha |\n| Q_(K,star) | beta |\n| Q_(R,star) | gamma |\n| Q_(K,0) | ell+eta |\n| Q_(0,K) | eta |\n| Q_(R,K) | beta+ell |\n| Q_(R,0) | R_flow=0 |\n\nAlso put weight1 on Q_(n,R), the actual last-step row. All unlisted\nweights are zero. It is convenient to retain the symbol R_flow in\nsome algebra so that its role as a row coefficient is explicit.\n\nThese weights are nonnegative. Indeed\n\n    theta=(U+e)/(U+2e+2w) <1,\n    theta>1/2 because U>2w,\n    alpha<1/8, beta>3/8, gamma>0, eta>0, kappa>1/2. (2.7)\n\nThe allowed chi_target=17/(16Z) is greater than chi0 because\nZ>17A^2. This follows from A<3/2 and Z>2n+1, n=2^m-1.\nThus there really is nonnegative objective slack available. The failures\nbelow take x_0=0 and therefore survive even arbitrarily large chi.\n\n## 3. Mechanism1a: exact lift with the actual last middle anchor\n\nRelease every middle gradient, keep q=g_K, and define only algebraically\n\n    E=sum_(i=1)^(K-1)h_i*(g_i-q).                 (3.1)\n\nThen\n\n    x_K=x_0-Ap-Mq-E,\n    x_R=x_0-Ap-Bq-E.\n\nKeep exactly the row weights of Section2. No interpolation row is placed\nat a mean. Relative to (2.4), the displacement terms of the affected\nrows are as follows:\n\n| Row | additional term |\n|---|---|\n| Q_(K,star) | <q,E> |\n| Q_(R,star) | <r,E> |\n| Q_(K,0) | <q,E> |\n| Q_(0,K) | -<p,E> |\n| Q_(R,K) | 0 |\n| Q_(R,0) | <r,E> |\n| Q_(0,star), Q_(n,R) | 0 |\n\nEvery f_i is still independent before cancellation. In particular there\nis no hidden equality f_K=f_1-M||q||^2. The coefficients of f_0,f_K,f_R\ncancel by the literal balance in Section2; all other intermediate\nsample values have coefficient0, not an invented value at a mean.\n\nConsequently (2.7) holds on the ENTIRE original formal space after adding\n\n    2<E,C>,\n    C=-eta*p+kappa*q+(gamma+R_flow)*r.            (3.2)\n\nThis is the exact frozen-weight remainder. Every internal gradient is\npresent in E, including g_1; the last middle endpoint remains q=g_K.\nThere is no positive ||E||^2 term.\n\nThis frozen lift is not PSD for any finite chi. Choose a unit vector z,\nx_0=0, p=q=r=z and g_n=-(A-1)z. Choose one actual internal i<K and\nset g_i=q-T*z/h_i, keeping the other middle gradients q. Then E=-Tz,\nC=z because -eta+kappa+gamma+R_flow=1, and the entire certificate is\n\n                         2D-2T.\n\nIt is negative whenever T>D. This is a FORMAL direction only: it is not\nclaimed to satisfy the interpolation inequalities. Its purpose is the\nfull-space PSD test required of a dual certificate. The available\nobjective slack cannot repair it because x_0=0.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1b: a legal fan of actual rows, with arbitrary probabilities\n\nA natural attempt to create internal squares is to replace the q-node\nrows by averages of ACTUAL query rows. Fix any probabilities omega_i>=0\nfor i in I, with sum omega_i=1. Retain alpha,beta,gamma,ell,eta,R_flow\nfrom Section2. Use the following original weights:\n\n    mu_(0,star)=alpha, mu_(R,star)=gamma,\n    mu_(i,star)=beta*omega_i,\n    mu_(i,0)=(ell+eta)*omega_i,\n    mu_(0,i)=eta*omega_i,\n    mu_(R,i)=(beta+ell)*omega_i          (i in I),\n    mu_(R,0)=R_flow,\n    mu_(n,R)=1.                                  (4.1)\n\nAll are explicit nonnegative original-row multipliers. There are no\nmiddle-to-middle rows in this particular fan. The balance at EVERY\nmiddle index is\n\n    beta*omega_i+(ell+eta)*omega_i\n                         -eta*omega_i-(beta+ell)*omega_i=0.\n\nThus all original sampled values cancel separately. No averaged value\nor fictitious jet is introduced. Define algebraic moments\n\n    P_i=sum_(l=1)^(i-1)h_l*g_l,  P_1=0,\n    dvec=sum_(i in I)h_i*g_i,\n    q_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*g_i,\n    N_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*||g_i||^2,\n    P_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*P_i,\n    T_omega=sum_(i in I)omega_i*<g_i,P_i>,\n    gbar_omega=alpha*p+beta*q_omega+gamma*r.         (4.2)\n\nThe term T_omega is chronologically ordered. It is not the square of a\nmean. Expanding every row in (4.1), using x_i=x_0-Ap-P_i and\nx_R=x_0-Ap-dvec, gives exactly\n\n    chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu Q\n      =chi||x_0||^2-2<gbar_omega,x_0>+2K_omega\n                                        +||g_n+(A-1)r||^2,       (4.3)\n\nwhere\n\n    K_omega=(alpha-eta*d)||p||^2+kappa*N_omega+w||r||^2\n       +[beta*A+ell*d+eta*(d-1)]<p,q_omega>\n       +[gamma*A+R_flow*d]<p,r>\n       +<r,dvec>-eta*<p,P_omega>\n       -(beta+ell)*<r,P_omega+q_omega>+kappa*T_omega.             (4.4)\n\nOne can check every coefficient directly as follows. The combined\ncoefficient of ||g_i||^2 is\n[beta+(ell+eta)+eta+(beta+ell)]omega_i/2=kappa*omega_i.\nThe coefficient of <g_i,P_i> is\n[beta+(ell+eta)]omega_i=kappa*omega_i.\nThe reverse rows Q_(0,i) contribute -eta<p,P_omega>; the Q_(R,i)\nrows contribute -(beta+ell)<r,P_omega+q_omega>. All rows involving\nx_R contribute exactly <r,dvec>, because\ngamma+beta+ell+R_flow=1. These exhaust internal occurrences.\n\nWhen omega is the point mass at K, q_omega=g_K and (4.4) reduces to\nSection3 including its displacement remainder. This independently\nchecks the actual endpoint orientation.\n\n## 5. Exact displacement-mean variance and chronological remainder\n\nNow use the concrete probabilities\n\n    omega_i=h_i/B,\n    q=q_omega=dvec/B,\n    nu_i=g_i-q, sum_(i in I)h_i*nu_i=0.            (5.1)\n\nThe symbol q here denotes only an algebraic displacement mean. The\nlegal proof expression is the original row sum (4.1), not an\ninterpolation row for q. Define\n\n    H2=sum_(i in I)h_i^2,\n    tau=(B^2-H2)/(2B), Delta_tau=tau-M,\n    Cum_i=sum_(l=1)^i h_l,\n    Z_chron=(1/B)*sum_(i in I)h_i*(B-Cum_i)*nu_i,\n    J2=sum_(i in I)h_i^2*nu_i,\n    V=sum_(i in I)h_i*||nu_i||^2.                 (5.2)\n\nThe exact moment identities are\n\n    N_omega=||q||^2+V/B,\n    P_omega=tau*q+Z_chron,\n    T_omega=tau*||q||^2-<q,J2>/B\n                         -sum_i h_i^2||nu_i||^2/(2B).           (5.3)\n\nProof: swap the finite sums defining P_omega to obtain its coefficient\nh_i(B-Cum_i)/B. For T_omega, each unordered pair appears once with\nweight h_i*h_l/B, so\n\n    T_omega=[||sum_i h_i*g_i||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2]/(2B).\n\nExpand g_i=q+nu_i and use the weighted zero-sum equation. This proves\n(5.3), including the term linear in J2; that term cannot be discarded.\n\nLet K_0(p,q,r) be the polynomial (2.4), evaluated algebraically at this\nmean q. Substitution in (4.4) gives the COMPLETE remainder\n\n    K_omega=K_0(p,q,r)+R_var,\n\n    R_var=Delta_tau*[kappa||q||^2-eta<p,q>-(beta+ell)<r,q>]\n       -eta<p,Z_chron>-(beta+ell)<r,Z_chron>\n       -(kappa/B)<q,J2>\n       +(kappa/(2B))*sum_i h_i*(2-h_i)*||nu_i||^2.                (5.4)\n\nThus the complete expression (4.3) is precisely (2.7), with q now\nthe algebraic mean, plus 2R_var. Every formal gradient, x_0 and the\nfree g_n remains present. No boundary square or common-gradient\ncharge is duplicated. The four types of lost terms are explicit:\n\n1. Delta_tau changes the sampled middle endpoint from physical mass M\n   to the average chronological location tau.\n2. Z_chron records the chronological order of every internal variation.\n3. J2 records the cross term between the mean and squared step weights.\n4. The remaining diagonal variance has signed weights h_i(2-h_i).\n\nThe first middle point is included with P_1=0; the last middle point is\nincluded with its actual h_K and prefix mass M. Its contribution to\nZ_chron has B-Cum_K=0. Original values disappear by separate balance,\nas shown before any mean was introduced. These observations account\nfor the endpoints and sampled values that a collapsed argument loses.\n\n## 6. Exact full-space obstruction to displacement-weighted row averaging\n\nSet x_0=p=r=g_n=0. In (4.3), before any mean substitution, the complete\ninternal principal form is\n\n    2*kappa*[sum_i omega_i||g_i||^2\n                         +sum_(l<i)omega_i*h_l<g_i,g_l>].       (6.1)\n\nFor omega_i=h_i/B, this is\n\n    (kappa/B)*[||sum_i h_i*g_i||^2\n                              +sum_i h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2].      (6.2)\n\nBoth formulas follow directly from (4.4) and do not assume any common\ngradient contact equations. In particular, the following obstruction\nalso applies to any outer coefficients of the same fan architecture\nwith kappa>0, whenever the displayed node balances hold.\n\nThere are two actual interior steps with h_i,h_j>2: choose the whole\nroot i=2^(m-1)-1 and the right-child root j=3*2^(m-2)-1. Both are in I,\nand h_i=1+a*c^(m-1), h_j=1+a*c^(m-2). Bounds (1.2), c>2 and m>=1536\ngive the strict inequalities h_i,h_j>2 without any numeric evaluation.\n\nFor a nonzero vector z set g_i=z/h_i, g_j=-z/h_j and all other middle\ngradients zero. Then sum h_l*g_l=0, and (6.2) equals\n\n    (2*kappa/B)*(1/h_i+1/h_j-1)*||z||^2 <0.        (6.3)\n\nThis is an exact full-space negative direction at every diagnostic m.\nNo x_0 allowance, including 17/(16Z), changes it. A merely positive\nvariance based on sum h_i||nu_i||^2 is not the actual row remainder.\nThe chronological part contributes the negative h_i^2 weights.\n\nFor arbitrary positive omega, a two-coordinate principal minor of\n(6.1), at l<i, also yields the necessary condition\n\n    omega_l >= (h_l^2/4)*omega_i.                 (6.4)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries, after removing the common positive factor,\nare omega_l,omega_i and its off-diagonal is h_l*omega_i/2. At the\ndisplacement probabilities this condition becomes h_l*h_i<=4, which\nfails for the two selected long steps. Non-displacement probabilities\nare NOT ruled out by this argument, and (6.4) is not sufficient for\ntheir full-space PSD. No adaptive probability family is asserted.\n\n## 7. Mechanism2: a concrete four-sector discriminator on the same word\n\nLet J=2^(m-1)-1 be the physical whole root, and split the actual middle\nqueries into I_L={1,...,J} and I_R={J+1,...,K}. Put\n\n    B_L=sum_(i in I_L)h_i, B_R=sum_(i in I_R)h_i, B=B_L+B_R,\n    V_L=sum_(i in I_L)h_i(2-h_i),\n    V_R=sum_(i in I_R)h_i(2-h_i).                 (7.1)\n\nThe four queried gradients are p at0, s on I_L, t on I_R, r atR.\nIts three genuine change locations are0,J,K. This discriminator is\nselected by the failed middle lift; it is not another proof of an\narbitrary checkpoint-pair subclass.\n\nBoth V_L and V_R are strictly less than -c^m. To prove this, note that\ntheir total positive linear contribution is at most2S<2c^m. I_L\ncontains the whole root, whose square exceeds c^(2m-2)/9 by a>1/3.\nI_R contains the right-child root, whose square exceeds c^(2m-4)/9.\nFor m>=9, c>2 gives c^(m-4)>2^5>27, and hence each of these root\nsquares exceeds3c^m. (7.2)\n\nTake a nonzero z and the formal assignment\n\n    p=r=x_0=g_n=0,\n    s=z/B_L, t=-z/B_R.                          (7.3)\n\nAll three queried changes are nonzero. Its full middle displacement\nis zero. On this FOUR-sector subspace the averaged certificate has\nthe strictly negative value\n\n    (kappa/B)*[V_L/B_L^2+V_R/B_R^2]*||z||^2.      (7.4)\n\nThus the obstruction is not dependent on isolated gradient spikes or\nan unbounded number of distinct queried gradients.\n\nCrucially, (7.3) is NOT a legal primal escape. The actual first step is\nx_1=x_0-Ap. The two original rows between0 and1 add to\n\n    Q_(0,1)+Q_(1,0)\n       =-d||p||^2+(d-1)<p,s>+||s||^2.           (7.5)\n\nAt (7.3) this is ||s||^2>0, contradicting interpolation. Equivalently,\nfor any legal first change, (7.5)<=0 is\n\n    ||s-(1-A/2)p||^2 <= (A^2/4)||p||^2,          (7.6)\n\nso p=0 forces s=0. The negative direction is excluded by a specific\noriginal omitted inequality. Moreover r=0 on any legal transcript\nwould mean the last query and terminal are minimizers, giving zero\nterminal objective. There is no asserted static function for (7.3).\n\n## 8. The single first-boundary-cycle repair also fails, with its price paid\n\nTest the concrete repair obtained by adding a nonnegative weight zeta\nto EACH of Q_(0,1) and Q_(1,0) in the mu convention. This leaves all\nvalues balanced and adds 2*zeta times (7.5) to the certificate. It is\nthe most direct actual-row repair of the violating pair just found.\nAll original fan weights are otherwise unchanged.\n\nOn (7.3), nonnegativity requires\n\n    zeta >= (kappa/(2B))*(-V_L-(B_L^2/B_R^2)*V_R)\n           > kappa/2 >1/4,                     (8.1)\n\nusing (7.2) and kappa>1/2.\n\nNow test a different formal vector in the SAME four-sector space:\nx_0=0, p nonzero, s=t=r=g_n=0. Nonnegativity requires\n\n    zeta <= (alpha-eta*d)/d < alpha/d\n           =A/(2D*d) <9/(4D)<1/8,               (8.2)\n\nbecause A<3/2,d>1/3,D>18. The initial\nnegative charge in the cycle is essential; treating it as a free\npositive ||s-p||^2 would conceal exactly this contradiction.\n\nThis is a scoped failure of one paid repair, not a proof that all\ncycles, all fan probability choices, or all original multipliers fail.\nAdding other rows could alter both tests. Such a new full-space\nconstruction has not been derived in this invocation.\n\n## 9. Complete original endpoint system for this four-sector transcript\n\nTo make the legality test explicit, here is its ENTIRE endpoint\ndescription, including all remote rows and the free terminal. Use\nsector labels a_s=0,1,2,3, with vectors\n\n    G_0=p, G_1=s, G_2=t, G_3=r,\n    L_0=A, L_1=B_L, L_2=B_R, L_3=A,\n    X_0=x_0, X_(a_s+1)=X_a_s-L_a_s*G_a_s.\n\nThe actual terminal position is x_n=X_4. Define the within-query\nendpoint masses\n\n    Tcap_0=0,\n    Tcap_1=B_L-h_J,\n    Tcap_2=B_R-h_K,\n    Tcap_3=0.                                   (9.1)\n\nLet F_a_s denote the value at the first point of sector a_s. They can be checked by (9.3)\non every physical sample. No feasibility or favorable objective is\nasserted merely from writing this finite system.\n\nFor context, if vectors and values DID satisfy the entire system,\naccepted1252 supplies a single static interpolant using all original\njets. With delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2 and y_j=x_j-g_j, including the zero\noptimum jet, it is\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_j[<y_j,v>-delta_j],\n    F(x)=sup_v[<x,v>-H(v)].                     (9.9)\n\nThe optimum rows ensure delta_j>=0. Thus H(0)=0, the active affine\npieces at0 are exactly those with delta_j=0, and its complete minimum\nset is\n\n    argmin F=partial H(0)=conv{y_j:delta_j=0}.    (9.10)\n\nThis last equality follows directly from the directional derivative of\na finite maximum at0, together with the zero derivative of ||v||^2/2;\nx is a minimizer of F exactly when0 maximizes <x,v>-H(v), equivalently\nx is a subgradient of H at0. The set includes0 from the optimum jet.\nIts true initial squared radius would be\n\n    dist(x_0,conv{y_j:delta_j=0})^2.             (9.11)\n\nThese formulas are an exact conditional completion/radius interface,\nNOT an initialized adversary or a radius value for (7.3). That formal\ndirection already fails (9.4) at the first boundary, so applying the\ninterpolant construction to it is unjustified.\n\n## 10. What a successful next lift must actually supply\n\nThe frozen and mean-weighted identities locate the missing terms rather\nthan suppressing them. Neither a diagonal-variance claim nor a choice\nof the available chi slack pays them.\n\nA new proof must give nonnegative original weights with exact value\nbalance and a full-space bound. It could change the middle probability\nlaw, add actual middle-to-middle rows, or recursively distribute flow,\nbut none of those mechanisms is completed here. For a fan with a\ndifferent probability law, even its internal two-coordinate blocks\nmust satisfy (6.4), and the chronological vectors P_omega,T_omega and\nthe independent displacement dvec still remain in (4.4). A successful\nrecursion cannot keep only the displacement mean while forgetting\nthese moments. Its boundary state and sign closure must be explicit.\n\nThe two formal tests in Section8 show why the first missing adjacent\ncycle cannot simply be assigned whatever weight repairs (7.4): its\ninitial charge must also be paid. They do not exclude a larger network\nthat supplies that price through other rows. No unspecified PSD block\nis offered as a theorem, and no unknown block has been assigned a\npositive sign.\n\nNo new substantive mechanism is begun after these two permitted ones.\n\n## 11. Literature grounding and retrieval limits\n\nOne Matlas query asked for a smooth-convex, nonconstant-stepsize\ninterpolation certificate with an arbitrary-gradient variance lift.\nIts three results did not provide that interface: their hypotheses\ninvolved a PL condition, stochastic subgradients, or short-step\nself-contractedness. They are not premises.\n\nThe fallback search identified Jason Altschuler and Pablo Parrilo,\n\"Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for\nSmooth Convex Optimization\", arXiv:2309.16530. The official PDF and\nHTML were downloaded under downloads/release_middle_sector/. Theorem\n3.4, its original gluing/rank-one/sparse corrections, Lemmas3.6--3.7,\nand the proof of the gluing theorem were read in extracted official\nHTML text. It does not\njustify replacing actual gradients by a sampled mean. Our obstruction\nconcerns the explicitly written fans (3.2),(4.1), not that gluing\ntheorem or all recursive certificates.
