{"id": "5f7612e07e36a926", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T19:52:46.870545+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2r74 complete JOINT UNVERIFIED report: explicit equal/unequal-radius three-generator cap-edge laws have genuinely R3 first gradients and positive initial cap height, yet their WHOLE same-m score is <beta^(2n)c^(-2m). Exact polar price, all normals and all physical prefixes retained. Pairwise cap-angle signs do not extend to active edge mixtures; long radius violates the old inequality. Broader post-first-step conic-absorption screen; original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Three-generator cap simplices: noninvariant starts and whole-word absorption\n\n**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN. No fact submission or new fact.**\n\nWorker max2, assigned invocation74, after ended baseline73. This is the\nsingle analytical invocation authorized by TASK.md and master guidance\nc0260879488ba7b6. The two selected laws below are equal radial lengths and\nunequal radial lengths. Both use three independently chosen positive heights,\nan explicit noninvariant start, an actual positive cap multiplier, and the\nentire original diagnostic word. Their complete parameter domains are\nobstructed. This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W and does not close\nall three-generator simplices.\n\n## 1. Unchanged target, scope, and candidate conclusion\n\nKeep the original PROBLEM.md, including W=2R, every horizon, and every\npredetermined nonnegative word. This investigation treats the SAME enclosing\ninteger m>=1536 and parameters\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m.\n\nEvery child retains this m, beta, c, and a. In particular h_0=1+a and\nh_1=1+beta. No leaf or terminal is deleted. Elementary bounds used below are\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 99/100<beta<1, 2<c<5/2.                 (1.1)\n\nThe symbol R in the initialization is a vertical depth, distinct from\nthe objective normalization R_n=W_n/2. Write P_E for Euclidean\nprojection onto a closed convex set E. All later local scalar and vector\nnotation is defined where introduced.\n\nFor the upper bound on c, use epsilon<1/100 and sqrt(2)<29/20, giving\nc<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2. Then a>(99/100)/(5/2)>1/3;\na<1/lambda<1/2. These are symbolic inequalities, not evaluated trajectories.\n\nThe horizontal directions and actual three-dimensional vertices are\n\n    z_0=(1,0), z_1=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), z_2=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),\n    v_i=(b_i*z_i,k_i), i=0,1,2,\n    D=conv{0,v_0,v_1,v_2}, K=cone{v_0,v_1,v_2},\n    F_D(Z)=max_(g in D){<Z,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nHere K is a geometric cone; it is distinct from the accepted lower-bound\nfunctional K_n. Write C_polar=K^polar. All spaces and all calculations are real\nEuclidean. The actual normalized payoff is\n\n    V_D(h,Z_0)=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2,\n    rho_0=dist(Z_0,C_polar), Z_(j+1)=Z_j-h_j*grad F_D(Z_j).\n\nLaw I: b_0=b_1=b_2=1.\nLaw II: b_0=B with 3<=B<=4, b_1=b_2=1.\nFor both laws choose independently\n\n    0<kappa<=a/1000,\n    k_i in [kappa,2*kappa] for i=0,1,2,\n    R in [kappa,2*kappa], t in [1/16,1/8].              (1.2)\n\nDefine\n\n    k_g=(1/2+t)*k_1+(1/2-t)*k_2,\n    e=(k_1-k_2)*(R+k_g)/sqrt(3),\n    Z_0=(-1/2-a/4, sqrt(3)*t+e, -R).                  (1.3)\n\nAll choices may depend on the whole known word and are fixed before running\none static function. Equations (1.2)--(1.3) are an explicit initialization,\nnot an optimization or a face-feasibility oracle.\n\nCandidate conclusion, proved jointly below for the WHOLE displayed domain:\n\n    0<=V_D(H_m,Z_0)< beta^(2n)*c^(-2m),\n    D_m*V_D(H_m,Z_0)<2*beta^(2n)*c^(-m)<2*c^(-m)<1.  (1.4)\n\nThe first three physical gradients have the strict supports\n\n    g_0 in relint conv{v_1,v_2}, with positive cap multiplier,\n    g_1 in relint cone{v_0,v_2}, with coefficient sum<1,\n    g_2 in relint cone{v_1,v_2}, with coefficient sum<1.\n\nThey are linearly independent. Consequently neither the gradients nor the\nactual iterates lie in a fixed two-dimensional affine evolution. Every\nremaining query and the actual terminal are controlled by a whole-history\nbound, not by extrapolating this three-point transition.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d defines COMPLETE K_n, including 1/D_m and every\nphysical single cut. Accepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n for\nthe COMPLETE alternating-ray channel. Thus (1.4) implies strict domination\nby both complete channels, without assuming either channel equals 1/D_m.\nAccepted 931b9653ce0f7201 gives only the UPPER benchmark comparison\nB_n/D_m<47/50. Its full-class hypothesis W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains\nunproved and is not inferred from this family calculation.\n\n## 2. Full Gram, independence, function, and whole minimum\n\nFor arbitrary positive b_i,k_i, put V=[v_0 v_1 v_2] and G=V^T V. Then\n\n    G_ii=b_i^2+k_i^2,\n    G_ij=-b_i*b_j/2+k_i*k_j, i!=j,\n    det V=(sqrt(3)/2)*(b_0*b_1*k_2+b_0*b_2*k_1+b_1*b_2*k_0)>0.\n\nExpansion along the horizontal coordinates proves the determinant formula.\nThus the vertices are independent, G is positive definite, and D is a\ngenuine origin-vertex tetrahedron. In particular its three-height model is\nnot a redundant two-generator representation. Its cone is closed: it is\nthe invertible linear image by V of the closed positive orthant.\n\nCompleting the square shows that the unique maximizer in F_D is the\nEuclidean projection g(Z)=P_D(Z). Its necessary and sufficient condition is\n\n    <Z-g,d-g><=0 for every d in D.                     (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating the squared distance along each\nsegment g+s(d-g); sufficiency follows by expanding ||Z-d||^2. Thus checking\nthe origin and all three vertices checks the entire body.\n\nAdd (2.1) at two points to obtain\n\n    ||g(Z)-g(Y)||^2<=<Z-Y,g(Z)-g(Y)>.\n\nIn particular g is 1-Lipschitz. The two defining maximum inequalities give\n\n    0<=F_D(Y)-F_D(Z)-<g(Z),Y-Z>\n       <=<g(Y)-g(Z),Y-Z><=||Y-Z||^2.\n\nTherefore F_D is differentiable, grad F_D=g, convex as a supremum of affine\nfunctions, and globally 1-smooth. It is everywhere finite because D is\ncompact. The presence of zero gives F_D>=0. Its COMPLETE minimizer set is\n\n    C_polar={Z:<Z,v_i><=0 for i=0,1,2}, min F_D=0.           (2.2)\n\nIndeed (2.2) makes every defining affine-quadratic value nonpositive. If\n<Z,v_i>>0 for some i, a sufficiently small positive multiple s*v_i in D\nhas value s<Z,v_i>-s^2||v_i||^2/2>0. This proves the converse.\n\nFor later use we also rederive cone projection and the actual closest\nminimum. Let u=P_K(Z). Applying its projection condition to 0 and 2u\nshows <Z-u,u>=0. Applying it to arbitrary points of K then gives\nr=Z-u in C_polar. Since <r,u>=0 and <p,u><=0 for every p in C_polar,\n\n    ||Z-p||^2=||u+r-p||^2>=||u||^2 for every p in C_polar,\n\nwith equality at p=r. Hence\n\n    P_Cpolar(Z)=Z-P_K(Z), dist(Z,C_polar)=||P_K(Z)||.             (2.3)\n\nThis proof supplies the full polar-distance identity used here; it is not\nimported from an unverified draft or an external theorem.\n\n## 3. Below-axis faces, including the missing cap normal guard\n\nThroughout an actual GD trajectory begun at Z_0=(X,-R), R>0, its vertical\ncoordinate remains negative. Every g in D has nonnegative vertical\ncoordinate, so, writing Z_j=(x_j,-r_j),\n\n    r_j=R+sum_(q<j) h_q*(g_q)_3>=R.                  (3.1)\n\nThis is actual vertical drift, not a frozen carrier coordinate.\n\nFor the cone, a below-axis point cannot project into its interior. An\ninterior projection would have zero residual by variation in every\ndirection, hence would equal Z, whereas every nonzero point of K has\npositive vertical coordinate. Thus P_K(Z) uses at most two generators.\n\nThere is also no full-cap interior projection of D at a below-axis point.\nLet w=V^(-T)*(1,1,1)^T, the normal with <w,v_i>=1. Since\n\n    sum_i v_i/b_i=(0,0,sum_i k_i/b_i),\n\none has\n\n    w_3=(sum_i 1/b_i)/(sum_i k_i/b_i)>0.               (3.2)\n\nIf g is in relint conv{v_0,v_1,v_2}, the residual in (2.1) must equal\nmu*w with mu>=0: its inner product with each v_i-g is zero, and its inner\nproduct with -g is nonpositive. Then Z_3=g_3+mu*w_3>0, a contradiction.\nThe interior of D is likewise impossible. A cap edge CAN be active; the\ninitial point in Section 6 checks all its normals explicitly.\n\nFor completeness the exact possible face formulas are as follows. Put\nq_i=<Z,v_i>. If J is a nonempty subset of {0,1,2} with |J|<=2, write\nG_J for its principal Gram and q_J for its score vector. A cone-face\ncandidate has\n\n    alpha=G_J^(-1)*q_J,\n    alpha>=0, sum alpha<=1,\n    q_l-G_(l,J)*alpha<=0 for l outside J.              (3.3)\n\nIt gives g=V_J*alpha and zero cap height. A cap-face candidate has\n\n    mu=[1^T G_J^(-1) q_J-1]/[1^T G_J^(-1)1],\n    alpha=G_J^(-1)*(q_J-mu*1),\n    mu>=0, alpha>=0, sum alpha=1,\n    q_l-G_(l,J)*alpha<=mu for l outside J.             (3.4)\n\nThe denominator is positive. These conditions are precisely all origin\nand vertex normals in (2.1). In (3.4), <Z-g,g>=mu; in (3.3) it is zero.\nAt boundaries there may be several valid supports but the projected\nvector is unique. The remaining case is g=0 when every q_i<=0. These\nformulas include vertices, conic partial faces, cap edges, and polar states.\nThey do not assert any face is reachable without its guards.\n\nOne can also write the general below-axis closest radius explicitly as\n\n    rho(Z)^2=max({0} union\n      {q_J^T G_J^(-1)q_J: 1<=|J|<=2, G_J^(-1)q_J>=0}). (3.5)\n\nTo justify (3.5), maximize 2<Z,u>-||u||^2 on K. Its global maximizer is\nP_K(Z), has at most two positive coefficients, and is stationary on their\nspan. Each displayed positive-coefficient candidate is a point of K, so\nits objective does not exceed the maximum. Conversely the actual support\nappears in the list. This is a finite closed geometry identity; the\ninitialization (1.3) will have a specified strict support and needs no\nselector or implicit feasibility problem.\n\n## 4. A uniform region where the finite cap cannot activate\n\nPut b_min=min_i b_i. For two distinct horizontal directions, positive\ncoefficients s,t give\n\n    ||s*b_i*z_i+t*b_j*z_j||^2\n      =(s*b_i)^2+(t*b_j)^2-s*t*b_i*b_j\n      =[(s*b_i+t*b_j)^2+3(s*b_i-t*b_j)^2]/4\n      >=b_min^2*(s+t)^2/4.                            (4.1)\n\nFor a single generator the same lower bound holds. Since a below-axis\ncone projection u has support size at most two, (4.1) proves\n\n    ||P_K(Z)||<=b_min/2  ==>  P_K(Z) in D.             (4.2)\n\nIndeed its coefficient sum is at most 2||u_horizontal||/b_min<=1.\nSince D is contained in K, a cone projection belonging to D is also the\nfinite-body projection. At every such point,\n\n    grad F_D(Z)=P_K(Z), F_D(Z)=||P_K(Z)||^2/2.          (4.3)\n\nFor a cone-active query write Z=u+r, u=P_K(Z), r in C_polar, <u,r>=0.\nFor a physical step h=1+d with d>=0,\n\n    Z-h*u=r-d*u,\n    dist(Z-h*u,C_polar)<=||d*u||,\n    ||P_K(Z-h*u)||<=d*||u||.                          (4.4)\n\nThe comparison point r belongs to the COMPLETE same polar C_polar. It is not\na reset minimum chosen for a child. Equations (4.2)--(4.4) will be used\njointly with a bound on EVERY intervening prefix product. A contraction\nat the completed horizon alone would not justify finite-cap inactivity.\n\n## 5. Exact products at every physical prefix of the original word\n\nFor integers 1<=q<=n let nu_2(q) be the exponent of 2 dividing q. The\nliteral recursive ruler word has\n\n    h_(q-1)-1=a*c^(nu_2(q))=beta*c^(nu_2(q)-1).        (5.1)\n\nProof: the middle index q=2^j of H_(j+1) has level j; its two children\nhave the earlier levels because nu_2(2^j+r)=nu_2(r) for 0<r<2^j.\nThis is an induction identifying physical indices, not a changed schedule.\n\nLet sbit(L) be the number of ones in the binary expansion of the integer\nL>=0. Counting each factor of 2 in 1,...,L gives\n\n    sum_(q=1)^L nu_2(q)=sum_(r>=1) floor(L/2^r)=L-sbit(L).\n\nThe last identity follows by writing L=sum_j e_j*2^j, e_j in{0,1}:\neach bit contributes sum_(r=1)^j 2^(j-r)=2^j-1. The sums are finite.\nConsequently, for EVERY 1<=L<=n,\n\n    product_(j=0)^(L-1)(h_j-1)=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),\n    product_(j=1)^(L-1)(h_j-1)\n      =beta^(L-1)*c^(1-sbit(L))<=1.                 (5.2)\n\nThe empty product at L=1 is one. At the ACTUAL terminal L=n=2^m-1,\n\n    product_(j=1)^(n-1)(h_j-1)=beta^(n-1)*c^(1-m).    (5.3)\n\nNo word, arithmetic product, or recurrence was executed by a program.\n\nCombine (4.2), (4.4), and (5.2). If the FIRST post-step state Z_1 obeys\n\n    ||P_K(Z_1)||<=b_min/2,                            (5.4)\n\nthen induction in the original physical index proves, for all 1<=L<=n,\n\n    g_L=P_K(Z_L) in D,\n    ||g_L||<=||P_K(Z_1)||*beta^(L-1)*c^(1-sbit(L)).    (5.5)\n\nThe induction also proves that the next GD step is the actual gradient\nstep of the one fixed F_D. It never postulates a cone trajectory and then\nnormalizes it. At L=n, g_n denotes the actual terminal gradient, although\nno gradient at n is used to take a step.\n\nThere is a useful broader consequence, independent of the explicit laws.\nFor any positive b_i,k_i, any below-axis start, and any actual closest\nminimum Z_star, firm nonexpansiveness with P_D(Z_star)=0 gives\n<Z_0-Z_star,g_0>>=||g_0||^2. Since 1<h_0=1+a<2,\n\n    ||Z_1-Z_star||^2\n       <=rho_0^2-h_0*(2-h_0)||g_0||^2<=rho_0^2.\n\nThus rho_1=||P_K(Z_1)||<=rho_0. If (5.4) holds, (4.3), (5.3) give\n\n    V_D(H_m,Z_0)<=beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-2m).               (5.6)\n\nThe estimate includes an initially positive cap multiplier. A cap-active\nstart alone therefore does not circumvent the product obstruction.\nFor all diagnostic m the bound in (5.6) is below 1/D_m, as Section 10\nshows. Consequently any three-generator below-axis witness that beats\n1/D_m must fail (5.4). This is a necessary screen, not a sufficiency\nclaim and not a closure of states outside that region.\n\n## 6. Explicit cap-edge initialization and all its normals\n\nWe now prove that the whole parameter domain (1.2)--(1.3), for either law,\nsatisfies the advertised strict transitions and (5.4). Write B=b_0, so\nB=1 in Law I and 3<=B<=4 in Law II. Both calculations below in fact hold\nfor 1<=B<=4. Since a<1/2, kappa<1/2000. Uniform elementary estimates are\n\n    kappa<=k_g<=2*kappa,\n    |e|<=4*kappa^2/sqrt(3)<4*kappa^2.                 (6.1)\n\nPut\n\n    g_0=(1/2+t)*v_1+(1/2-t)*v_2\n       =(-1/2,sqrt(3)*t,k_g),\n    N_0=Z_0-g_0=(-a/4,e,-R-k_g).\n\nBoth cap barycentric coefficients are strictly positive. Directly,\n\n    <N_0,v_1-v_2>=sqrt(3)*e-(R+k_g)*(k_1-k_2)=0,\n    delta_0=<N_0,g_0>\n       =a/8-[(k_1+k_2)/2]*(R+k_g)\n       >=a/8-8*kappa^2>0.                            (6.2)\n\nThus both active vertex normals are zero and the origin normal equals\n-delta_0<0. At the remote vertex,\n\n    <N_0,v_0>=-B*a/4-k_0*(R+k_g)<0,\n    <N_0,v_0-g_0><-delta_0<0.                         (6.3)\n\nAll of (2.1) is checked, hence this is exactly the first physical gradient\nand a strictly positive queried cap height. It is not a formal face alone.\nThe first actual state is\n\n    Z_1=(a/4,-a*sqrt(3)*t+e,-R-(1+a)*k_g).            (6.4)\n\nLet x_1 be its horizontal part and r_1 its positive depth. Using\nsqrt(3)<7/4, t<=1/8, and kappa<=a/1000,\n\n    ||x_1||<=a/4+a*sqrt(3)/8+4*kappa^2\n             <15a/32+4*kappa^2<a/2,\n    0<r_1<5*kappa.                                   (6.5)\n\nThe last inequality in the first line follows from 128*kappa^2<a,\nwhich follows from kappa^2<=a^2/1000000 and a<1/2.\n\nAt any below-axis point (x,-r), a nonzero cone projection u obeys\n\n    ||u||^2=<Z,u>=<x,u_horizontal>-r*u_3\n             <=||x||*||u||.\n\nThus ||P_K(Z)||<=||x||, also when u=0. Equation (6.5) proves the strictly\nstronger bound\n\n    ||P_K(Z_1)||<a/2<1/2=b_min/2.                    (6.6)\n\nThis already establishes whole-word cap inactivity after the first query\nby Section 5. The next section verifies the claimed changes of conic\nprojection and excludes a hidden invariant plane.\n\n## 7. Strict two-face transitions and genuine three-dimensionality\n\nFor a generator pair J={i,j}, its span is the graph\n\n    (y,eta_J dot y),\n    eta_J dot (b_i*z_i)=k_i, eta_J dot (b_j*z_j)=k_j.\n\nThe relevant slope vectors are\n\n    eta_12=(-k_1-k_2,(k_1-k_2)/sqrt(3)),\n    eta_02=(k_0/B,-(2*k_2+k_0/B)/sqrt(3)).             (7.1)\n\nThey satisfy ||eta_J||<5*kappa for 1<=B<=4. For Z=(x,-r) the orthogonal\nprojection onto this plane has horizontal coordinate\n\n    y=x-tau*eta_J,\n    tau=(r+eta_J dot x)/(1+||eta_J||^2).               (7.2)\n\nIts vertical coordinate is eta_J dot y, and its residual is\n tau*(eta_J,-1). In particular\n\n    ||y-x||<=5*kappa*(r+5*kappa*||x||).               (7.3)\n\nIf the horizontal coefficients on the two generators are positive, the\nprojected vertical coordinate is positive. Then tau=r+g_3>0. For the\nomitted index l, the exact remote guard is\n\n    (eta_J,-1) dot v_l\n       =-b_l*sum_(i=0)^2 k_i/b_i<0.                  (7.4)\n\nEquation (7.4) follows by taking its inner product with sum_i v_i/b_i.\nThus positivity of the pair coefficients proves every cone normal, the\norigin equality, and the strict omitted-generator guard. It is not an\nassumption that an arbitrary plane projection lies on its cone face.\n\n7a. First post-step projection has support {0,2}.\n\nFor J={0,2}, (6.5) and (7.3) give ||y-x_1||<40*kappa^2. Since the\nerror e in (6.4) has magnitude<4*kappa^2,\n\n    ||y-(a/4,-a*sqrt(3)*t)||<50*kappa^2.              (7.5)\n\nFor any horizontal y its coefficients on B*z_0,z_2 are\n\n    alpha_2=-2*y_2/sqrt(3),\n    alpha_0=(y_1-y_2/sqrt(3))/B.\n\nAt the reference point in (7.5) these are 2a*t>=a/8 and\n a*(1/4+t)/B>=5a/64. Each changes by less than 100*kappa^2 under the\nstated error. The inequality 100*kappa^2<a/64 proves that both are\nstrictly positive. Hence (7.4) applies and the plane projection is g_1.\nIts coefficient sum is less than a by (4.1) and (6.6), so the finite cap\nis strictly inactive. Also\n\n    (g_1)_3=eta_02 dot (g_1)_horizontal\n       <=5*kappa*||g_1||<(5a/2)*kappa<5*kappa/4.      (7.6)\n\nA sharper bound ||eta_02||<=4*kappa follows directly from (7.1):\n(k_0/B)^2<=(2*kappa)^2 and\n(2*k_2+k_0/B)^2/3<=(6*kappa)^2/3. We will use it to obtain\n\n    (g_1)_3<2a*kappa<kappa.                           (7.7)\n\n7b. The next projection has support {1,2}.\n\nThe actual second step is h_1=1+beta. Put Z_2=Z_1-(1+beta)*g_1.\nLet tau_1 be (7.2) at Z_1 with J={0,2}. Writing\ng_1 horizontal=x_1-tau_1*eta_02 and using the <40*kappa^2\nplane error above gives\n\n    ||(Z_2)_horizontal-(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t)||\n       <=beta*|e|+(1+beta)*40*kappa^2<100*kappa^2.     (7.8)\n\nConsequently ||(Z_2)_horizontal||<a; this follows from (6.5) and\n100*kappa^2<a/2. Its depth is\n\n    r_2=r_1+(1+beta)*(g_1)_3<7*kappa.                 (7.9)\n\nProject onto the plane {1,2}. By (7.3), (7.8), (7.9), and a<1/2,\n\n    ||(g_2)_horizontal-(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t)||\n          <150*kappa^2,                              (7.10)\n\nprovided this plane projection is valid; we now check that validity.\nThe horizontal coefficients on z_1,z_2 are -y_1+y_2/sqrt(3) and\n-y_1-y_2/sqrt(3). At the reference point they are\n\n    beta*a*(1/4+t), beta*a*(1/4-t)>=beta*a/8.\n\nTheir errors are less than 300*kappa^2. They remain positive because\n\n    300*kappa^2<3a/20000<a/32<beta*a/16.\n\nThus (7.4) proves the claimed projection and its strict remote guard.\nIts coefficient sum is less than one by the already proved (5.5)--(6.6).\nNo finite-cap edge is silently reached in this second projection.\n\n7c. The first three gradients span the real ambient R^3.\n\nBoth g_0 and g_2 lie in span{v_1,v_2}. At zero error the horizontal\n2-by-2 determinant is\n\n    det((-1/2,sqrt(3)*t),(-beta*a/4,beta*a*sqrt(3)*t))\n       =-beta*a*sqrt(3)*t/4.\n\nIts magnitude is at least beta*a*sqrt(3)/64>a/128. Since\n||(-1/2,sqrt(3)*t)||<1, (7.10) changes this determinant by less than\n150*kappa^2<3a/40000<a/256. It is therefore strictly negative.\nHence g_0,g_2 are independent in that generator plane. The vector g_1\nhas a strictly positive v_0 coefficient, while V is invertible. Thus\n g_0,g_1,g_2 are linearly independent. Because the first three steps are\npositive, the first four actual states cannot all lie in a proper affine\nplane. This conclusion is uniform over every parameter allowed in (1.2).\n\n## 8. Actual closest initial minimum, explicitly priced\n\nAt Z_0 the relevant cone projection has strict support {1,2}. To prove\nthis without guessing the minimum, use (7.2) for eta_12. The horizontal\nnorm of Z_0 is less than one: a/4<1/8, sqrt(3)*t<1/4, and\n|e|<4*kappa^2<1/8. Its depth is at most 2*kappa. Thus its plane\nprojection u_0 has horizontal error less than 35*kappa^2 relative to\n(Z_0)_horizontal, and less than 50*kappa^2 relative to\n\n    x_0^ref=(-1/2-a/4,sqrt(3)*t).\n\nAt this reference point its coefficients on z_1,z_2 are\n\n    1/2+a/4+t, 1/2+a/4-t>=3/8.\n\nTheir errors are less than 100*kappa^2<1/8, so they are strictly\npositive. Equations (7.2), (7.4) now give the true cone projection and\nstrict remote v_0 guard. With x_0=(Z_0)_horizontal, put\n\n    tau_0=(R+eta_12 dot x_0)/(1+||eta_12||^2)>0,\n    y_0=x_0-tau_0*eta_12,\n    u_0=(y_0,eta_12 dot y_0),\n    Z_star=tau_0*(eta_12,-1).                         (8.1)\n\nEquation (2.3) proves that Z_star is the unique closest point in the\nWHOLE polar, including its remote inequality. The actual radius is\n\n    rho_0^2=||y_0||^2+(eta_12 dot y_0)^2\n           =||Z_0||^2-(R+eta_12 dot x_0)^2/(1+||eta_12||^2)\n           =q_12^T*G_12^(-1)*q_12,                  (8.2)\n\nwhere q_12=(<Z_0,v_1>,<Z_0,v_2>)^T. All quantities are the explicitly\nchosen input parameters. Neither ||Z_0|| nor R is substituted for the\nclosest radius.\n\nIn particular <Z_0,g_0>=||g_0||^2+delta_0>||g_0||^2.\nSince Z_star in C_polar, <Z_star,g_0><=0, and Cauchy--Schwarz yields\n\n    rho_0>||g_0||>=1/2.                              (8.3)\n\nThere is also no hidden radial clipping at the first cap query. The\nhorizontal determinant of g_0 and u_0 has reference value\n a*sqrt(3)*t/4>=a*sqrt(3)/64>a/64. Its error is less than\n50*kappa^2<a/128. Therefore u_0 and g_0 are not parallel. The actual\nfinite projection cannot be obtained by radially clipping P_K(Z_0).\nThis is an exact property of the initialized legal point, not a proposed\nasymptotic model.\n\n## 9. Every original point, remote row, and actual terminal\n\nEquations (5.5), (6.6) show that for EVERY original index 1<=j<=n,\n\n    g_j=P_K(Z_j)=P_D(Z_j),\n    ||g_j||<(a/2)*beta^(j-1)*c^(1-sbit(j)),\n    delta_j=<Z_j-g_j,g_j>=0.                         (9.1)\n\nThe states are exactly Z_j=Z_0-sum_(q<j)h_q*g_q with the depth (3.1).\nFor each j, the unique cone projection is given by (3.3) without the\nfinite-cap constraint, which (9.1) proves automatically. It may have\nany valid single/pair support or may be zero; no future face is silently\nprescribed. At the ACTUAL terminal,\n\n    g_n=P_K(Z_0-sum_(q<n)h_q*g_q) in D,\n    F_D(Z_n)=||g_n||^2/2,\n    ||g_n||<(a/2)*beta^(n-1)*c^(1-m).                (9.2)\n\nThus the terminal has an exact projection formula and a proved global\nbound. The report is not merely an unanalysed finite projection recurrence.\nAll intermediate gradients and their normals follow from a uniform\ninduction; no numerical replay, reset, expected face, or projected-history\nnormalization is used.\n\nFor arbitrary original indices i,j, including both terminal orientations,\nlet f_i=F_D(Z_i). Their ordered normalized interpolation residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,Z_j-Z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<Z_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                      (9.3)\n\nThe equality is direct algebra using f_i=<Z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The\ninequality is the full projection normal at j. It applies also at the\ninitial cap query, whose origin and remote guards are (6.2)--(6.3).\nFor the actual closest optimum,\n\n    Q_(i,star)=<Z_star,g_i><=0,\n    Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.                           (9.4)\n\nThe whole Gram of all original positions and gradients is positive\nsemidefinite because all are actual vectors in R^3; no formal directions\nare identified. Translating positions by Z_star preserves these facts.\nFor any L>0 take f=L*F_D and steps h_i/L. This preserves every state and\nthe payoff 2(f(Z_n)-min f)/(L*rho_0^2)=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2. This is the\nrequired W=2R normalization. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is consistent with\nthis row convention; its terminal reduction is not used to discard a row.\n\n## 10. Entire-word payoff and complete-channel comparison\n\nUsing the actual minimum and radius (8.2)--(8.3), (9.2) gives\n\n    V_D=||g_n||^2/rho_0^2\n       <a^2*beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-2m)\n       =beta^(2n)*c^(-2m).                           (10.1)\n\nThis holds for every parameter in both laws, with no asymptotic parameter\nqualification. To compare with 1/D_m, rederive the literal mass identity.\nPut A_j=1+S_j and v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. Then\n\n    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j,\n    A_j=(1-v)*2^j+v*c^j.\n\nSubstitution proves the last equality. Since c>lambda>2,\n c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1, so 0<v<1. Therefore A_m<c^m and\n\n    D_m=2A_m-1<2*c^m.                               (10.2)\n\nCombining (10.1)--(10.2) proves (1.4). In particular the supremum over\nthe WHOLE parameter law is at most beta^(2n)c^(-2m), and its ratio to\nthe complete K/ray channels is at most\n\n    D_m*beta^(2n)*c^(-2m)<2*c^(-m),                  (10.3)\n\nwhich tends to zero as m tends to infinity. This is a quantitative\nwhole-law failure, stronger than absence of a demonstrated gain.\n\nFor the broader absorption screen (5.6), (10.2) gives\n\n    D_m*V_D<2*beta^(2n-2)*c^(2-m)<1\n\nfor all diagnostic m; for instance m>=4 and c>2 already suffice. Thus\npositive cap height at the first query, followed by (5.4), is universally\ninsufficient within this geometric model, even without the small-height\nboxes or the particular starts of the two laws.\n\nThe accepted complete channels obey\n\n    1/D_m<=K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m).\n\nWe do not replace either complete channel by a selected checkpoint or\nassert its exact value. By accepted931b9653ce0f7201 the initialized laws\nalso obey B_n*V_D<(47/50)*2*c^(-m). The benchmark inequality is used\nin its correct upper direction. It supplies no full-W upper certificate.\n\n## 11. What actually fails in a pairwise cap-angle extension\n\nThe old two-ray envelope uses both inequalities\n\n    kappa_angle*C<=O, kappa_angle*O<=C,\n\nwhere C,O are the two cap lengths and kappa_angle is the negative cosine\nof their obtuse angle. For positive length generators v_i,v_j, these are\nexactly\n\n    -<v_i,v_j><=||v_j||^2,\n    -<v_i,v_j><=||v_i||^2.                            (11.1)\n\nIn Law I every generator pair satisfies both strictly: its left side\nis 1/2-k_i*k_j<1/2, whereas each squared norm is at least one.\nBut the ACTUAL initial cap-edge gradient g_0 satisfies\n\n    -<v_0,g_0>=1/2-k_0*k_g,\n    ||g_0||^2=1/4+3t^2+k_g^2,\n    -<v_0,g_0>-||g_0||^2\n        =1/4-3t^2-k_0*k_g-k_g^2\n        >=13/64-8*kappa^2>0.                        (11.2)\n\nThus (11.1) is not preserved by replacing a generator with a cap-edge\nmixture, even when that mixture is an actual original physical gradient.\nIn addition Section 8 proved g_0 is not parallel to the cone projection.\nThese statements refute the proposed generator-pair-to-radial-clipping\nextension. They do NOT refute every conceivable scalar or multidimensional\nenvelope; a different full inclusion and objective comparison would need\nits own proof. We do not silently use pairwise signs as such a proof.\n\nA concrete discriminator on EVERY original H_m is obtained by taking\n\n    B=1, kappa=a/1000,\n    (k_0,k_1,k_2)=(kappa,3*kappa/2,2*kappa),\n    R=kappa, t=1/16,\n\nand precisely (1.3). Every original normal, the strict cap query, the\nthree-dimensional transition, complete polar, exact closest price (8.2),\nand the ENTIRE-word score (10.1) have been proved for this member. This\nis not an isolated two-point counterdirection. Its normalized score is\nsmall, so failure of that local proof extension does not imply a useful\nhard-function gain.\n\nLaw II also breaks a generator-pair inequality itself. For the short\nvertex v_1 and long vertex v_0,\n\n    -<v_0,v_1>-||v_1||^2\n      =B/2-1-k_0*k_1-k_1^2\n      >=1/2-8*kappa^2>0.                            (11.3)\n\nWith C=||v_1||, O=||v_0|| and\nkappa_angle=(B/2-k_0*k_1)/(C*O)>0, equation (11.3) is precisely\n kappa_angle*O>C, the failure of the second necessary inequality.\nAll normals and prices remain paid, and the same whole-law obstruction\n(10.1) applies. Breaking this angle inequality is therefore insufficient\nfor this explicit law too. It is not a universal insufficiency theorem\nfor unequal radial dictionaries.\n\n## 12. Boundaries, open interface, and author audit\n\nThe useful new interface is the finite-cap absorption condition (5.4),\nwith a whole-history prefix-product proof. Equal radial lengths and three\nindependent heights do permit nonradial clipping and genuinely\nthree-dimensional changes. Unequal radii can violate the exact old cap\ninequality. Nevertheless these initialized trajectories enter a region\nwhere every later finite cap is inactive and the entire-word score is\nuniformly negligible. This conclusion covers the whole two displayed\nparameter laws and, via (5.6), a larger explicitly stated absorption\nregion. It is NOT a closure of arbitrary starts, large heights, recurrent\ncap edges outside the absorption region, or all higher-dimensional\nsimplices.\n\nThe still-open productive interface is an explicit whole-word law that\nescapes (5.4), pays its true polar radius, and keeps enough positive\nqueried cap multipliers to avoid the product loss. Merely breaking a\npairwise sign, exhibiting a new face, or showing local noncommutation\ndoes not do this. No recurrent-cap law outside the absorbed domain has\nbeen initialized or bounded here. Even a positive diagnostic witness\nwould leave the original every-n/every-word target open.\n\nAuthor audit of load-bearing points:\n\n* The first finite cap is reached by all origin and remote normal checks.\n* The full-cap interior is excluded using its actual positive vertical\n  normal (3.2), not by picturing a planar cross-section.\n* The conic and finite projections are distinguished; they are provably\n  not collinear at Z_0 and become identical only after the proved entry.\n* Both strict post-step face guards include the omitted generator.\n* g_0,g_1,g_2 span R^3 uniformly, so the laws do not reduce to an old\n  invariant two-ray or two-generator-face trajectory for the whole word.\n* The initial closest minimum is (8.1) in the WHOLE polar; (8.2) is the\n  exact price. The lower bound (8.3) is used only in an upper obstruction.\n* The proof bounds every intervening prefix product, not just the full\n  product. Thus finite-cap membership is justified throughout.\n* The terminal is the actual n-th state, its gradient is its actual\n  projection, and all row orientations including optimum rows are paid.\n* All newly needed geometry, cone, and ruler claims are rederived in this\n  one joint proof. Older drafts are awareness, not accepted premises.\n* This audit is by the author and is not independent verification.\n\n## 13. Independent large-curvature objective and original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Clipped scalar gradients: all common curvatures and an independent-curvature obstruction\n\n**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,\nworker max3, invocation78 after ended77, completed ceiling78 / attempted79,\ncontroller cap1. The original unrestricted minimax target remains OPEN.\n\n## 1. Results and exact scope\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 and, throughout every child, use\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2*S_m,\n    n_m=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=a*beta.\n\nThese are local hierarchy symbols. In particular b is a plateau factor,\nnot either clipping level. d_m is an auxiliary radius, not another step.\nThe largest actual level is m-1. All original physical steps, including\nthe final level-zero leaf ell, are retained.\n\nFor b_plus,b_minus>0 and kappa_plus,kappa_minus in[0,1], define ONE static\nfunction on the whole line by\n\n    g(x)=min(kappa_plus*x,b_plus), x>=0,\n    g(x)=max(kappa_minus*x,-b_minus), x<=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.\n\nLet Psi_j(s) be its actual full-word GD output from s using H_j,\nfor0<=j<=m, including the identity Psi_0.\nThe principal new candidate is the ENTIRE common-curvature law:\n\n    kappa_plus=kappa_minus=kappa in(0,1] implies\n    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n          = kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m).                    (1.1)\n\nThis is for EVERY fixed positive pair b_plus,b_minus, all starts, and\narbitrary dependence of parameters on m. It includes every effective-step\nregime kappa*h<1, =1, between1 and2, =2 and >2. The supremum is attained on\neach saturated ray. Taking the supremum also over common kappa gives1/D_m.\n\nFor independent positive curvatures put K=max(kappa_plus,kappa_minus), and\n\n    kappa_0=1/(1+d_(m-2)).\n\nA second exact candidate covers the complete low-curvature region:\n\n    0<K<=kappa_0 implies\n    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n          = K/(1+2*K*S_m).                            (1.2)\n\nEvery trajectory here has at most one sign change, since only the root\ncan cross zero. Section12 proves this using the two different curvatures,\nnot by replacing the function with a common-curvature function.\n\nWhen exactly one curvature is zero and the other is kappa>0, the exact\npositive-own-distance profile is kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m), for every\nnonnegative physical word, not just H_m. If both vanish the objective is\nzero; no quotient by a zero closest distance is taken.\n\nThe remaining independent-positive-curvature region is\n\n    K>kappa_0, kappa_plus!=kappa_minus, both positive, (1.3)\n\nwith all clipping levels and starts retained. No full profile is asserted\nthere. Section13 gives a legal genuinely clipped full-word counterexample\nto transplanting the common-curvature radial lemma using K. Its exact\nobjective is below1/D_m, so it refutes that intermediate reduction, not\nthe desired objective bound or the original minimax statement.\n\nThe original normalization remains W_n(h)=sup 2(F(x_n)-F_star)/\n(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), with W=2R and the original all-n/all-nonnegative-h\nquantifiers. A restricted scalar family upper bound is not an upper bound\non full W. Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 is used only for its unconditional\nB_(n_m)/D_m<47/50; its full-W hypothesis17/(16D_m) stays unproved. Prior\nunit-core draft987e49ed35eb24e7 was read as awareness; all premises needed\nhere are derived jointly. No assertion from the unclipped two-ray draft\nbf10ce01c54882c8 is imported.\n\n## 2. Static legality, all rows, and closest distance\n\nFor a ray with positive curvature kappa_sigma, let\nu_sigma=b_sigma/kappa_sigma. With r=|x| on that ray,\n\n    f(x)=kappa_sigma*r^2/2, 0<=r<=u_sigma,\n    f(x)=b_sigma*r-b_sigma^2/(2*kappa_sigma), r>=u_sigma.\n\nFor zero curvature that entire ray has f=g=0. The derivative g is\ncontinuous and nondecreasing. On every linear piece its slope is in[0,1].\nPartitioning any interval at the finite breakpoints proves\n|g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable and globally\n1-smooth. For arbitrary advertised L>0, use F=L*f and physical steps h/L;\nthe scalar iterates are unchanged and the normalized objective is the\nsame.\n\nThe complete minimum set C is {0} when both curvatures are positive;\n(-infinity,0] when only kappa_plus is positive; [0,infinity) when only\nkappa_minus is positive; and the whole line when both are zero. The minimum\nvalue is0. When the closest initial distance is positive it is exactly\n|s| in the first three cases. Starts already in C are stationary and have\nzero gap. In the identically zero case every original admissible pair has\nzero payoff, while the closest-distance quotient is left undefined.\n\nFor every physical index, including the unqueried terminal, define\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n_m.\n\nEvery ordered original interpolation row follows directly. For x<y put\nDelta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. For x<=t<=y, monotonicity and smoothness\ngive\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).\n\nIntegrating each inequality gives at least Delta^2/2. These are precisely\nthe two ordered inequalities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.       (2.1)\n\nEqual locations give equality. This treats every remote pair, every\nrevisit, all threshold equalities, the terminal, and all minimizers.\nThe full Gram matrix of the actual scalar displacement and gradients is\npositive semidefinite because each quadratic form is a square of a real\nlinear combination. No formal gradient coordinate or row is removed.\n\nSpatial scaling multiplies both clipping levels and the initial position\nby the same factor and leaves curvature unchanged. Removing a common\ncurvature from g instead changes EVERY effective step. Section3 does that\nchange explicitly; no word is silently replaced or reset.\n\n## 3. Common curvature: effective word and constants\n\nFor common kappa>0 put u=b_plus/kappa and v=b_minus/kappa, reflect if\nnecessary so u>=v, and write f=kappa*F, where\n\n    G(x)=F'(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]).\n\nThe actual update becomes x -> x-alpha_i*G(x), with alpha_i=kappa*h_i\nat physical index i. Separately define the following level quantities:\n\n    delta=1-kappa, R_j=kappa*d_j,\n    t_j=kappa*(1+d_j)=kappa+R_j,\n    e_j=t_j-1=R_j-delta,\n    M_j=kappa*S_j, C_j=1+M_j=kappa*A_j+delta.\n\nHere t_j is a level-j effective bridge, not physical time. The effective\nword is exactly kappa*H_m. Its masses obey\n\n    C_(j+1)=2*C_j+e_j, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+t_j,\n    R_(j+1)=c*R_j.                                  (3.1)\n\nUsing epsilon<=1/6144 and sqrt(2)<17/12 gives\n\n    12/5<c<49/20, beta>99/100,\n    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.             (3.2)\n\nFor the lower bound on b, a>99/245 and therefore\nb>9801/24500>2/5. The upper bounds use c>lambda and beta<1.\nLet eta=a/(c-2). Since c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1,\n0<eta<1. Summing the literal word gives\n\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    A_j>=d_j/(c-2), A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2.                                (3.3)\n\nThe first identity follows from A_0=1 and A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\nusing2*eta+a=c*eta. Consequently\n\n    M_j>=R_j/(c-2)-kappa,\n    e_j/C_j<=d_j/A_j<1/2 whenever e_j>=0.            (3.4)\n\nAlso, with the local constant\n\n    Q=1/(c-2)+1/c,\n\none has Q>1160/441>5/2>c. No evaluated hierarchy or numerical estimate\nis used in these inequalities.\n\nEvery level-k occurrence in H_m has a literal H_k immediately before and\nafter it, so its physical prefix and suffix masses are at least S_k.\nAfter its following H_k, the remaining suffix consists of increasing\nancestor levels, each followed by its full right child:\n\n    H_k, t_(l_1),H_(l_1), ..., t_(l_r),H_(l_r),\n    k<l_1<...<l_r<=m-1.                              (3.5)\n\nThe H symbols in this display have their effective steps kappa*H. This\nis a decomposition of the actual binary tree: ascend past right-child\nancestors until reaching the next left-child ancestor, then take its\nbridge and right child. At k=0 the first H_0 is empty. At the final leaf\nthere is no remaining component.\n\n## 4. Exact signed step images and a monotone-block bound\n\nFor common curvature, an interval[-p,q] contains0 and its image under\na step t remains an interval containing0. If0<=t<=1, the map is\nnondecreasing, and the exact endpoint magnitudes are\n\n    p'=p-t*min(p,v), q'=q-t*min(q,u).                 (4.1)\n\nIf t>=1, writing e=t-1, the exact endpoint magnitudes are\n\n    p'=max(e*min(q,u),p-t*v),\n    q'=max(e*min(p,v),q-t*u).                        (4.2)\n\nFor(4.2), each ray has slope-e in its core and slope1 on its tail;\nits extrema are at a threshold or an endpoint. The first terms in the\nmaxima are nonnegative. Formulae(4.1)-(4.2) coincide at t=1 and include\nall endpoint/threshold equalities. At t=2 the core slope is-1, and for\nt>2 it is less than-1; neither case is omitted.\n\nWrite Phi_j(p,q) for the TWO nonnegative endpoint magnitudes of the\nexact image Psi_j([-p,q]). Continuity makes both endpoints attained.\nInterval inclusion makes Phi_j coordinatewise nondecreasing. Composition\nwith(4.1) or(4.2) is exact for the whole interval. Bounds below therefore\napply to compatible actual child histories, not independently chosen\nchild maximizers. This interface is used only symbolically.\n\nConsider any block all of whose effective steps lie in[0,1], of total\nmass M. Sign is preserved, and on a ray with threshold w its radius map\nis a composition of r -> r-t*min(r,w). Each such map is convex and\nnondecreasing on r>=0, fixes0, and has slopes1-t,1. The complete map is\ntherefore convex, nondecreasing and fixes0. In particular\n\n    B(theta*r)<=theta*B(r), 0<=theta<=1.             (4.3)\n\nThere is also an explicit upper bound by the clipped continuous flow:\n\n    B(r)<=V_M(r;w),\n    V_M(r;w)=r*exp(-M),                       r<=w,\n             r-M*w,                         r>=w and M<=r/w-1,\n             w*exp(r/w-1-M),                 r>=w and M>=r/w-1. (4.4)\n\nHere(4.4) is an inequality proved from the displayed elementary maps,\nnot an invocation of an external flow theorem. One step stays linear\non a tail until the threshold. A crossing step has residual radius\nw*(1-z)<=w*exp(-z), z>=0. A core step satisfies1-t<=exp(-t).\nThe displayed maps V form a semigroup by direct substitution and are\nnondecreasing in their input. Induction over the block proves(4.4).\nIncreasing the ray threshold can only decrease the radius map for these\nnondecreasing steps, so a smaller comparison threshold is legitimate.\n\nIf the terminal radius exceeds w, all queried radii are at least that\nterminal radius, and every gradient radius is at least w. Hence\n\n    B(r)<=max(w,r-M*w).                              (4.5)\n\nThis last assertion also follows directly from the physical updates.\n\n## 5. All common curvatures: the starting level for plateaus\n\nThe plateau factor is the SAME b from(3.2). Define a starting level J.\nIf kappa*ell>=1, set J=1. If kappa*ell<1, let J be the smallest positive\ninteger with R_J>=delta, if such an integer is needed within the word.\nThen\n\n    delta<=R_J<c*delta,\n    every physical step in H_J is less than1.       (5.1)\n\nIf no such J occurs before m, the entire word is sign preserving and is\nalready covered by Section9. Whenever this section is used, J<=m.\nFor all j>=J the bridges have e_j>=0 and e_j<=R_j.\n\nThe base plateau is, for every integer r>=0,\n\n    Phi_J(R_J*w*b^r,R_J*w*b^r)\n              <=(w*b^(r+1),w*b^(r+1)).             (5.2)\n\nHere choose w=u for1<=u/v<=2, and w=v for u/v>=2.\nWe prove both choices directly.\n\nFirst take kappa*ell<1. Put R=R_J and M=M_J. By(3.4),(5.1),\n\n    M>=R/(c-2)-kappa, delta>R/c,\n    M>=R/(c-2)-1+delta.                              (5.3)\n\nFor w=v, both ray thresholds are at least v, so(4.4) applies with\ncomparison threshold v. If R<=1, the output divided by v is at most\n\n    R*exp(-M)<=exp(1)*R*exp(-Q*R)<=1/Q<2/5<b.\n\nThe middle function has derivative exp(1-Q*R)*(1-Q*R), so its global\nmaximum is1/Q at R=1/Q. If R>=1, (5.3) gives M>R-1, and the output\nis at most exp(R-1-M)<exp(-(Q-1)*R)<2/5. The last bound follows from\nQ>5/2, R>=1, and exp(z)>=1+z. This proves(5.2) at r=0.\n\nFor w=u and u/v<=2, both thresholds are at least u/2. Compare with\nthreshold u/2. If R<=1/2 the same R*exp(-M) bound is below b. If\nR>=1/2, (5.3) gives M>2*R-1, and the output divided by u is at most\n\n    (1/2)*exp(2*R-1-M)\n       <(1/2)*exp(-(Q-2)*R)\n       <=(1/2)/(1+1/4)=2/5<b.\n\nHere Q-2>1/2 and R>=1/2. Convexity(4.3), separately on each ray,\nnow proves(5.2) for all r>=0 in BOTH cases.\n\nIf kappa*ell>=1, put e_0=kappa*ell-1 in[0,a]. Also R_1=kappa*beta<1.\nFor w=v the entire base interval is in the common core, so both endpoint\nmagnitudes are e_0*R_1*v*b^r<=a*beta*v*b^r=b*v*b^r.\nFor w=u and u/v<=2, apply(4.2). The core extremum is at most\ne_0*R_1*u*b^r<=b*u*b^r. The only additional tail term is\n\n    R_1*u*b^r-t_0*v\n       <=b^r*(R_1*u-t_0*v)\n       <=b^r*u*kappa*(beta-ell/2)<=b^(r+1)*u.\n\nIndeed beta-ell/2<=b, since\nb-beta+ell/2=(1+a)/2-beta*(1-a)>(3*a-1)/2>0;\nif beta-ell/2 is negative the desired upper bound remains immediate.\nThis proves the base plateau in every case, including kappa*ell=1.\n\nFor the large-ratio proof we also need the positive endpoint at the\nlarger base radius:\n\n    second coordinate of Phi_J(c*R_J*v,c*R_J*v)<=v. (5.4)\n\nIn the low-curvature base, negative inputs stay negative. A positive\ninput has threshold at least2v. If c*R<=2, (4.4) bounds the positive\nendpoint divided by v by\nexp(1)*c*R*exp(-Q*R)<=c/Q<1. If c*R>=2, it is bounded by\n\n    2*exp(c*R/2-1-M)\n       <2*exp(-(Q-c/2)*R)\n       <=2*exp(-(2*Q/c-1))<2/exp(1)<1.\n\nThe same inequalities show that the threshold is reached before the\nend; Q>c is sufficient. In the high-curvature base, a negative input\ncan return to at most e_0*v<=a*v. A positive core input has nonpositive\noutput. A positive saturated input at most c*R_1*v has output at most\nkappa*(c*beta-2*ell)*v<0, because c<49/20 and2*ell>14/5.\nThus(5.4) holds in this case too.\n\n## 6. Ratio at most two: exact capture and all higher plateaus\n\nAssume1<=u/v<=2. For every j<=m,\n\n    Psi_j([-C_j*v,C_j*u])=[-v,u].                   (6.1)\n\nAn exterior start x>=C_j*u stays positive saturated at every query\nand ends at x-M_j*u; the negative exterior has the analogous affine\nformula. Indeed each physical prefix has mass at most M_j, leaving\nradius at least its threshold. This proves the exterior assertion\nwithout any sign assumption on core slopes.\n\nFor(6.1), the empty child is the identity. If(6.1) holds for j, the\nfirst child of the parent interval has exact endpoint magnitudes\n(C_j+t_j)*v and(C_j+t_j)*u, by the just proved exterior formula and\nits central capture interval. If t_j<=1, (4.1) sends these to C_j*v\nand C_j*u. If t_j>=1, (4.2) does the same: its cross terms satisfy\ne_j*u<=C_j*v and e_j*v<=C_j*u by e_j/C_j<1/2 and u/v<=2. The\nsecond child then gives exactly[-v,u]. This completes the induction.\n\nWe extend(5.2) to every j>=J, with w=u. Suppose it holds for j.\nFor the unscaled parent radius c*R_j*u, (6.1) and its exterior\nbranches bound the first-child endpoints by\n\n    p<=max(v,c*R_j*u-M_j*v),\n    q<=max(u,c*R_j*u-M_j*u).\n\nThe bridge has e_j>=0. Its core cross terms are at most R_j*u.\nIts negative tail term is at most\nmax(-e_j*v,c*R_j*u-(M_j+t_j)*v), and its positive tail term is at\nmost max(-e_j*u,(c*R_j-M_j-t_j)*u). Both are at most R_j*u because\n\n    M_j+t_j=kappa*A_j+R_j\n              >=(1+1/(c-2))*R_j,\n    (c-1)*R_j*(u/v)<=2*(c-1)*R_j<=M_j+t_j.\n\nThe last inequality uses c-2<1/2. Thus the ACTUAL bridge image is\ncontained in[-R_j*u,R_j*u], and the second child's plateau bounds\nboth terminal endpoints by b*u.\n\nFor r>=1, the parent radius is\nc*R_j*u*b^r=(c*b)*R_j*u*b^(r-1)<=R_j*u*b^(r-1).\nThe first-child plateau bounds its image by u*b^r on both sides.\nSince2*b<1, this interval is inside both core thresholds. The bridge\nmultiplies absolute values by e_j<=R_j. The second child's plateau\nat r then gives u*b^(r+1) on both sides. This proves\n\n    Phi_j(R_j*u*b^r,R_j*u*b^r)\n                  <=(u*b^(r+1),u*b^(r+1))          (6.2)\n\nfor every j>=J and every integer r>=0.\n\n## 7. Ratio at least two: joint drift, returns and plateaus\n\nAssume u>=2v. For every j>=J, prove together\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=max(v,|s|-M_j*v/3) for every real s, (7.1)\n    Phi_j(R_j*v*b^r,R_j*v*b^r)\n                  <=(v*b^(r+1),v*b^(r+1)), r>=0.   (7.2)\n\nThe base plateau was proved in Section5. If the base block has steps\nless than1, compare both rays with the smaller threshold v, as justified\nafter(4.4), and apply(4.5). This proves(7.1). For the high-curvature leaf base,\na negative-to-positive output is at most e_0*v<v. A positive output\nlarger than v must come from a positive saturated input and loses t_0*u.\nA negative output-p with p>v, from a negative input, loses t_0*v.\nFrom a positive input s it satisfies p<=e_0*s. If e_0=0 this is\nimpossible; otherwise e_0<=a<1/2 gives\ns-p>[(1-e_0)/e_0]*v>v>t_0*v/3, since t_0<3/2.\nThese cases prove the base drift.\n\nWe first describe a consequence of already proved lower-level plateaus.\nAny negative-to-positive crossing at level k>=J lands at most e_k*v\n<=R_k*v. Its following actual H_k captures it to b*v. Each later\nancestor in(3.5) sees radius at most b*v<v, has core multiplier\nof magnitude e_l<=R_l, and its H_l captures it again to b*v.\nIf the high-curvature case permits a level-zero crossing, its landing\nis at most e_0*v<=a*v<v. If it is the final leaf that is the actual\nterminal. Otherwise its next ancestor has level at least1 and the\nsame plateau argument applies. In the low-curvature case levels k<J\ncannot cross at all. Thus whenever the needed proper-child plateaus\nare available, a negative-to-positive return implies terminal radius\nat most a*v. A zero hit stays zero. This consequence is used only with\nlevels strictly below the block whose drift or plateau is being proved.\n\nIn particular, for a child H_j with j>J, the positive endpoint at input\nradius c*R_j*v is at most a*v. A trajectory with a return has that bound.\nWithout such a return, any positive terminal must come from an entirely\npositive trajectory. Its root level j-1 has t_(j-1)>=1. Remaining positive\nthere forces a saturated root query, and all earlier positive queries\nare saturated because positions decrease. Its initial s must satisfy\n\n    s>=(M_(j-1)+t_(j-1))*u\n       >=2*(1+1/(c-2))*R_(j-1)*v\n       >c^2*R_(j-1)*v=c*R_j*v.                     (7.3)\n\nThe strict comparison follows from c^2<25/4 and\n2*(1+1/(c-2))>58/9>25/4. This contradicts the input radius.\nFor j=J the necessary bound by v is(5.4). Therefore in EVERY induction\nstep the first child's positive endpoint is at most v.\n\nSuppose both(7.1)-(7.2) hold at j. Write R=R_j, M=M_j, t=t_j,\ne=e_j. At parent input radius c*R*v, the first child has\n\n    p<=max(v,(c*R-M/3)*v), q<=v.\n\nAlso p<=(kappa+2*R)*v. Indeed kappa+2*R>=1 because R>=delta, and\n\n    (c-2)*R<=kappa+M/3\n\nfollows from M>=R/(c-2)-kappa and(c-2)^2<1/3. Formula(4.2) now\nputs BOTH bridge endpoint magnitudes at most R*v: e*q<=R*v,\np-t*v<=R*v, e*min(p,v)<=R*v, and q-t*u<=0. The second child's\nplateau proves the parent plateau at r=0. For r>=1 the geometric\nargument of Section6 applies with w=v: c*b<1 places the first child\nin its previous radius, its image is in the common core, and e<=R\nallows the second plateau. Thus(7.2) is proved at j+1 BEFORE its drift.\n\nFor the parent drift write y=Psi_j(s), z=y-t*G(y), w=Psi_j(z).\nOnly |w|>v needs proof. If w>v, the return consequence, using the\nalready available proper-child plateaus, excludes any negative-to-\npositive crossing. The whole trajectory is then positive. Its gradient\nat every query is at least min(w,u)>v, so\ns-w>=M_(j+1)*v, stronger than required.\n\nLet w=-p with p>v. Child drift and child plateau give respectively\n\n    |z|>=p+M*v/3, |z|>R*v.                          (7.4)\n\nThere are three possible bridge cases.\n\nA. If z>0, a negative y could land at most e*v<=R*v, contradicting\n(7.4). Thus y>0 stays positive across the bridge. Because t>=1,\nthis query is saturated and y=z+t*u>u. The first child's terminal y>u\nexcludes a return; all of its queries are positive saturated. Hence\ns=z+(M+t)*u>=p+M*v/3+(M+t)*v>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.\n\nB. If z<0 and y<0, a core input would be nonnegative after the bridge.\nThus the query is negative saturated, |y|=|z|+t*v>v, and applying child\ndrift to the first child yields\n|s|>=|y|+M*v/3>=p+2*M*v/3+t*v>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.\nThe inequality |y|>v also follows from R+t=kappa+2*R>=1.\n\nC. If z<0 and y>0, then |z|<=e*min(y,u). By(7.4), e<=R implies\ny>v and min(y,u)>v. The first-child output y>v excludes returns, so\nthat entire child is positive and\n\n    s>=y+M*min(y,u).\n\nIf y<=u, then |z|=e*y and\ns-|z|>=(1+M-e)*y. If y>=u, then |z|=t*u-y and\ns-|z|>=2*y+(M-t)*u>=(1+M-e)*u. In either case\n\n    3*(1+M-e)-(M+t)\n       =6-4*kappa+2*M-4*R\n       >=6*delta+2*R*(1/(c-2)-2)>=0.                (7.5)\n\nIn particular1+M-e>0, and s-|z|>=(M+t)*v/3.\nCombining with(7.4) gives s>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.\n\nZero bridge inputs or outputs cannot occur in these cases. The cases\nare exhaustive. Since M_(j+1)=2*M+t, they prove(7.1) at j+1 and\ncomplete the joint induction. All child histories and both endpoints\nremain actual. No unit-core positive-tail assertion was used.\n\nFor the FULL H_m, the same physical suffix argument now gives\n\n    a negative-to-positive crossing => |Psi_m(s)|<=a*v. (7.6)\n\nThe final leaf uses a*v, not b*v; it has not been deleted.\n\n## 8. Radial consequence and exact overlap of curvature ranges\n\nEither plateau(6.2) with w=u or(7.2) with w=v implies\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/R_j for0<|s|<=R_j*w, j>=J.      (8.1)\n\nChoose an integer r>=0 such that\nR_j*w*b^(r+1)<|s|<=R_j*w*b^r. The plateau bounds the output by\nw*b^(r+1)<|s|/R_j. If an amplitude is a geometric boundary, take it\nas the upper boundary of its next cell. Zero maps to zero. This covers\nevery real amplitude in the specified interval, not a sampled mesh.\n\nWe need the final comparison\n\n    R_m^2>=1+2*kappa*S_m.                            (8.2)\n\nIt holds for EVERY kappa>=kappa_0=1/(1+d_(m-2)). Here is an explicit\nuniform overlap proof. Let d=d_(m-2). By a>2/5,c>2,m>=1536, d>20,\nso(d/(1+d))^2>9/10. By A_m<c^m and d_m=c^2*d,\n\n    (kappa_0*d_m)^2>(9/10)*c^4,\n    1+2*kappa_0*S_m<1+2*c^2/a<1+5*c^2.\n\nFor x=c^2>144/25>23/4, the polynomial(9/10)*x^2-5*x-1 is positive:\nit equals1/160 at23/4 and has positive derivative for x>=23/4.\nThus(8.2) holds strictly at kappa_0. For larger kappa the equivalent\ninequality d_m^2>=1/kappa^2+2*S_m/kappa only becomes weaker.\nThis proves(8.2) throughout the advertised range.\n\nAlso J exists below m in this range: at kappa_0,\nR_(m-1)=c*d/(1+d)>1, and it increases with kappa. Since delta<1,\nthe low-curvature starting level, if needed, is at most m-1.\nNo unproved asymptotic overlap or rounding choice is hidden here.\n\n## 9. All no-crossing and single-crossing histories\n\nThis section is independent of clipping ratio. Consider a segment of\ncommon-curvature effective mass M, staying on one ray sigma, with initial\nradius r and terminal radius w. Write F_sigma(w)=F(w) on the positive\nray and F_sigma(w)=F(-w) on the negative ray. Its radii are nonincreasing and\nr>=w+M*min(w,u_sigma). Directly from the objective pieces,\n\n    2*F_sigma(w)<=r^2/(1+2*M).                       (9.1)\n\nIf w<=u_sigma, use w<=r/(1+M) and(1+M)^2>=1+2*M.\nIf w>=u_sigma, use r>=w+M*u_sigma and\n\n    (w+M*u_sigma)^2\n       -(1+2*M)*(2*u_sigma*w-u_sigma^2)\n          =(w-(1+M)*u_sigma)^2>=0.\n\nThese arguments include a zero terminal and equality at the threshold.\n\nSuppose exactly one sign crossing occurs at level k, at a physical\nposition whose effective prefix and suffix masses are P,T. Let y>0\nbe its pre-crossing radius, after reflection if necessary. The entire\nprefix stayed on its ray, so with q=min(y,u_source),\n\n    |s|>=y+P*q>=(1+P)*q,\n    crossing radius r=t_k*q-y<=e_k*q\n                         <=e_k*|s|/(1+P).          (9.2)\n\nA crossing requires e_k>0. The suffix stays on the other ray and (9.1)\napplies there. It remains to prove the exact positional inequality\n\n    e_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2*T)]<=1/(1+2*M_m).           (9.3)\n\nPut C=C_k>=1, e=e_k>0, and N=M_m-M_(k+1)>=0. For some x in[0,N],\nP=M_k+x, T=M_k+N-x. The denominator in(9.3) is\n\n    (C+x)^2*(2*C-1+2*N-2*x).\n\nIts logarithm is concave, so its minimum occurs at an endpoint. The\nendpoint x=N minus the endpoint x=0 equals\nN*(2*C*(C-1)+N*(2*C-1))>=0. Thus the denominator is at least\nC^2*(2*C-1+2*N), while1+2*M_m=4*C+2*e-1+2*N.\nBy e/C<1/2,\n\n    e^2*(4*C+2*e-1)/(C^2*(2*C-1))\n       <(5*C-1)/(4*(2*C-1))<=1,\n\nthe last inequality being3*(C-1)>=0. Adding the2*N terms preserves\nthe inequality because e^2<C^2. This proves(9.3) for every position,\nincluding both endpoint leaves. Combining(9.1)-(9.3) and f=kappa*F\ngives score at most kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m).\n\nIf kappa<=kappa_0, every non-root step has effective size at most1.\nSuch a step preserves sign globally by(4.1). The only possible crossing\nis at the root, so this section proves the desired upper bound for the\nENTIRE common-curvature family in that region. Root and threshold\nequalities cause no exception; a zero hit remains zero.\n\n## 10. Complete common-curvature proof and attainment\n\nIt remains to cover kappa>=kappa_0. Then(8.2) holds and all plateaus\nneeded at m exist.\n\nFor1<=u/v<=2, take a start in the capture rectangle of(6.1). Starts outside\nthe capture rectangle follow their exact saturated affine branches;\n(9.1), or its displayed square identity, gives the required bound.\n\nFor u/v>=2, if |s|<=R_m*v use(8.1) exactly as just done. If the\ninitial radius is larger and a negative-to-positive crossing occurs,\n(7.6) gives score at most kappa*a^2/R_m^2, also below the desired\nbound. Without a negative-to-positive crossing, a nonzero trajectory\nhas no sign change or has exactly one positive-to-negative crossing.\nSection9 covers both. A trajectory hitting zero has zero gap.\n\nThe two clipping-ratio domains cover every ratio after reflection. The\nlow- and high-curvature domains overlap at kappa_0 with the exact\ncomparison in Section8. Thus every kappa in(0,1], all clips, both signs,\nand all amplitudes are included.\n\nFor attainment, let D_kappa=1+2*kappa*S_m and choose s=D_kappa*u.\nAt every actual index i, with physical prefix mass P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,\n\n    x_i=u*(D_kappa-kappa*P_i)>=u,\n    x_(n_m)=u*(1+kappa*S_m),\n    2*f(x_(n_m))=kappa*D_kappa*u^2.\n\nThe own initial radius is D_kappa*u and the attained score is\nkappa/D_kappa. Choosing s=-D_kappa*v gives the reflected attainment.\nThese are queries of the one fixed function in Section2 and satisfy\nall its ordered rows. This proves the joint candidate(1.1).\n\nAs a function of kappa, the now-proved profile kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m)\nhas positive derivative1/(1+2*kappa*S_m)^2. On a zero-curvature ray Stay_sigma(r)=r.\nFor a true interval[-p,q], the exact next signed endpoint magnitudes are\n\n    p'=max(Stay_minus(p),Cross_plus(q)),\n    q'=max(Stay_plus(q),Cross_minus(p)). TASK.md and master_guidance53db4a56c9e0379b\nauthorize one invocation, at most two substantive mechanisms, and zero\nmathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays,\noptimizers, solvers, formal calls, installations, consults or subagents.\nThose counts remain zero. Only textual mathematics and administrative\nreading, writing, retrieval and hashing were used.\n\n## 1. Outcomes and limits\n\nMechanism I strengthens the previous two literal-skew exclusions. For the\nENTIRE family\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, alpha_i=(1-p)u_i/U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0), kappa>0, tau>=0,\n\nno balanced skew re-selection can produce a full certificate at\nq<=qtar=17/(16D).
