{"id": "7662d3077f76bf26", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T20:53:07.783295+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "JOINT UNVERIFIED max2r75: all-start centered equilateral-triangle profile has an explicit finite algebraic max formula (degree at most8), with every original query/terminal guard and actual radius. Full initial core and every full fixed-edge/vertex class are derived; the latter have exact optimum1/D_m. Uniform comparison for all changing-face histories remains OPEN; no arbitrary-N or full-W result.", "evidence": "# Full initial-state centered-triangle profile: explicit finite formula and unresolved comparison\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation75 after ended baseline74.\nProject gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance c0b98bc7d6b0f588.\nOne analytical invocation, at most two substantive mechanisms. No new fact\nis asserted: the formal gate is closed. All new arguments below form ONE\njoint draft and do not use unverified sibling conclusions as premises.\nOriginal target OPEN.\n\n## 0. Verdict and exact scope\n\nThe body is exactly the assigned centered equilateral triangle in its actual\nEuclidean plane. Every nonzero initial point, every original step, and the\nactual terminal are included in the finite formula in Sections 7--10.\nNo scalar-axis extremality, fixed support, or selected inverse branch is\nassumed in that formula.\n\nThe draft derives:\n\n* Unique minimum0, smoothness1, complete projection guards and every original\n  ordered interpolation row, including terminal rows.\n* All starts in the entire triangle remain in the quadratic core through\n  every physical prefix. Their normalized terminal payoff is exactly\n  beta^(2n)c^(-2m).\n* Each of the three full fixed-vertex classes and each of the three full\n  fixed-edge classes has exact supremum1/D_m, attained. These classes include\n  arbitrary permitted transverse coordinates, not only their axes.\n* The unrestricted triangle supremum is attained. An explicit finite\n  expression for it uses affine products, linear sign tests, and roots of\n  polynomials of degree at most two; there is no residual continuous\n  optimization, existence-of-transcript test, or unsolved matrix inequality.\n  Its value is algebraic of degree at most8 over the rationals for each m.\n* A concrete legal original-word prefix refutes the simple inradius-based\n  radial drainage inequality that would otherwise have closed the comparison.\n\nThe finite expression is NOT evaluated, and its comparison with1/D_m or\n17/(16D_m) is NOT proved. In particular, this is not a claim that the triangle\nhas exact optimum1/D_m, nor that it gives a nonvanishing improvement. The\nfirst mechanism yields the geometric screens and obstruction; the second\neliminates the continuous variables in the full profile. No arbitrary-N\nsimplex theorem is claimed. The assignment permits a second triangle method\nwhen the decisive first-method comparison remains unresolved, and that is\nhow the second mechanism is used here.\n\nThe exponential face list is a literal finite algebraic expression, not a\nproposal to run an exhaustive search. No part of that list was enumerated or\nevaluated by a mathematical program. It does not resolve the original\nall-dimension/all-word minimax problem.\n\n## 1. Parameters and body\n\nFix one integer m>=1536 throughout. Define\n\n  epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n  c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n  n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, D_m=1+2S.\n\nAll children use these SAME parameters. The word has exactly n physical\nsteps, numbered0,...,n-1. Put h_i=(H_m)_i. Its last step is1+a.\nLet\n\n  v_1=(1,0), v_2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v_3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),\n  D=conv{v_1,v_2,v_3},\n  F(x)=max_(g in D) [x dot g-||g||^2/2],\n  x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i),\n  E_m(x_0)=2F(x_n)/||x_0||^2, x_0!=0,\n  V_triangle(m)=sup_(x_0!=0) E_m(x_0).\n\nAll norms and inner products are the usual real Euclidean ones. The three\nvertices have norm1, sum0, and pairwise inner product-1/2. Consequently\n\n  D={x: v_i dot x>=-1/2 for i=1,2,3},\n  {x: ||x||<=1/2} subset D subset {x: ||x||<=1},\n  -D subset 2D.\n\nFor each edge opposite v_i put n_i=-v_i, t_i=J n_i, where\nJ(u_1,u_2)=(-u_2,u_1), and b=sqrt(3)/2. Its endpoints are the two\npoints (1/2)n_i +/- b t_i.\n\nThe dihedral group generated by permutations of the vertices preserves D,\nF, the GD maps, and E_m. This symmetry is recorded but is NOT used to replace\nan arbitrary initial point by a symmetry axis.\n\n## 2. Legality, full minimum, metric, and all original rows\n\nCompleting the square shows that the maximizer in F is the unique Euclidean\nprojection g=P_D(x). The defining projection inequality is\n\n  (x-g) dot (z-g)<=0 for every z in D.                 (2.1)\n\nIt follows either by differentiating the squared distance along the segment\nfrom g to z, or conversely by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Thus (2.1)\nis necessary and sufficient and supplies a direct check of every guard below.\n\nFor two inputs x,y and projections g,p, add (2.1) with z=p and z=g to get\n\n  ||g-p||^2<=(g-p) dot (x-y)<=||g-p|| ||x-y||.\n\nHence projection is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give, for an\nincrement u,\n\n  g dot u <= F(x+u)-F(x) <= P_D(x+u) dot u.\n\nTheir difference is at most||u||^2, proving differentiability with gradient\ng and smoothness1. Convexity follows from F being a maximum of affine\nfunctions of x. The function is finite everywhere because D is compact.\n\nSince0 belongs to D, F>=0 and F(0)=0. For x!=0 choose\n0<t<min{1,1/(2||x||)}. Then tx belongs to D and\nF(x)>=(t-t^2/2)||x||^2>0. Thus the complete minimum set is exactly{0}.\nThe true initial distance is ||x_0||; there is no unused carrier or selectable\ncloser minimum.\n\nFor all original samples i=0,...,n let g_i=P_D(x_i), f_i=F(x_i), and add\nthe optimum sample x_*=g_*=0,f_*=0. Set z_j=x_j-g_j. Since\nf_i=x_i dot g_i-||g_i||^2/2, direct expansion gives, for EVERY ordered pair,\n\n  Q_ij := f_i-f_j+g_i dot(x_j-x_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        = (g_i-g_j) dot z_j <=0.                     (2.2)\n\nThe last inequality is (2.1) for g_j and z=g_i. This includes every pair\nwith the terminal n and every pair involving *. No reduced row family or\nterminal replacement is being used.\n\nFor any spatial scale s>0,\nF_(sD)(x)=s^2 F_D(x/s). Thus the run from s x_0 is the scale-s version\nof this run, and its normalized payoff is identical. Multiplication of F\nby any L>0, with steps h_i/L, also preserves the points and normalized\npayoff. The report uses the original W=2R normalization throughout.\n\n## 3. Seven closed projection regions, with every guard\n\nThere are seven face labels: C for the whole triangle, E_i for an edge,\nand V_i for a vertex. Associate to each a symmetric orthogonal projection\nmatrix P_f, vector b_f orthogonal to its range, and closed region R_f:\n\n  C:   P_C=I, b_C=0,\n       R_C={x: -v_i dot x<=1/2 for all i};\n\n  E_i: P_E=t_i t_i^T, b_E=n_i/2,\n       R_E={x: -n_i dot x<=-1/2,\n                  t_i dot x<=b, -t_i dot x<=b};\n\n  V_i: P_V=0, b_V=v_i,\n       R_V={x: (v_j-v_i) dot x<=-3/2 for both j!=i}.\n\nOn R_f the actual projection is\n\n  P_D(x)=P_f x+b_f,                                 (3.1)\n  2F(x)=x^T P_f x+2b_f dot x-||b_f||^2.            (3.2)\n\nFor C this is x itself. For E_i write x=z n_i+u t_i: the guards are\nz>=1/2, |u|<=b, and (3.1) is n_i/2+u t_i. Its residual is\n(z-1/2)n_i and (2.1) follows from n_i dot g<=1/2 on D. For V_i,\n(2.1) for the other two vertices is exactly the two displayed guards;\nlinearity gives it for their entire convex hull. Conversely each projection\nlies in the interior of D, in an edge, or at a vertex, giving one of these\nseven regions by (2.1). Shared boundaries are permitted: the formulas agree\nthere because the projection is unique. These facts prove both coverage\nand the absence of a spurious branch.\n\nFormula (3.2) follows by inserting (3.1) into x dot g-||g||^2/2 and using\nP_f^2=P_f=P_f^T and P_f b_f=0. It remains valid at every shared boundary.\n\nFor orientation only, an alternative full two-coordinate description uses\nq_i=(2/3)x dot v_i, with sum q_i=0 and\n||x||^2=(3/2)sum q_i^2. If A=max q_i and B=-min q_i, then\nA/2<=B<=2A and the ordered triple is(A,B-A,-B). The core guard is\nB<=1/3; the vertex guard is2A-B>=1; otherwise the two upper coordinates\nform the active edge. The next ordered triple must actually be sorted\nagain after an overrelaxed step. Nothing in this report presumes that its\nold order persists or that equal coordinates can split.\n\n## 4. Every physical prefix and the complete initial core\n\nFor a positive integer q let nu_2(q) be the largest integer r>=0 for which\n2^r divides q. For an integer ell>=0 let sbit(ell) be the number of ones\nin its binary expansion. The original ruler word satisfies\n\n  h_(q-1)-1=a c^(nu_2(q))=beta c^(nu_2(q)-1),\n                      1<=q<=n.                    (4.1)\n\nIndeed the bridge of H_(j+1) is at q=2^j, and for0<r<2^j the right\nchild has nu_2(2^j+r)=nu_2(r). This proves (4.1) by induction on the\nliteral concatenation.\n\nFor every physical prefix length1<=ell<=n,\n\n  sum_(q=1)^ell nu_2(q)=sum_(r>=1) floor(ell/2^r)\n                     =ell-sbit(ell),\n  p_ell:=product_(i=0)^(ell-1)(h_i-1)\n         =beta^ell c^(-sbit(ell))<=beta/c=a<1/2.      (4.2)\n\nTo prove the second counting identity write ell as sum e_j2^j. Each bit\ncontributes sum_(r=1)^j 2^(j-r)=2^j-1 to the floor sum. All sums are finite.\nThe bound uses beta<1, sbit(ell)>=1, and c>1+sqrt(2)>2.\n\nSet p_0=1 and p=p_n=beta^n c^(-m). If x_0 belongs to D, then\n\n  x_ell=(-1)^ell p_ell x_0, 0<=ell<=n.               (4.3)\n\nThis is a genuine induction on the physical updates: for every nonempty\nprefix a positive scale <=a maps D into D, while a negative scale of\nmagnitude <=a maps D into2aD subset D. Thus each point predicted by (4.3)\nis in D, its actual projection is itself, and the next update gives (4.3)\nagain. This checks every intervening prefix, including those containing\nlarge bridges. It is not an inference from the final product alone.\n\nConsequently every nonzero x_0 in D has\n\n  E_m(x_0)=p^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                     (4.4)\n\nFor later comparisons derive the mass formula within this joint draft.\nPut S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, and v_mass=a/(c-2). The recursion gives\nA_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j and hence\n\n  A_j=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass c^j.                     (4.5)\n\nSubstitution checks the initial value and every step. Since\nlambda(lambda-2)=1, c>lambda and beta<1 imply0<v_mass<1. For j>=1,\nA_j<c^j. Therefore D_m=2A_m-1<2c^m<c^(2m), and\n\n  p^2<1/D_m.                                      (4.6)\n\nThe middle strict inequality uses c^m>2.\n\n## 5. Full fixed-face classes: exact terminal scores and own radii\n\nA fixed-face class here requires the projection to belong to the designated\nclosed face at every original query AND at the actual terminal. Shared\nboundary labels cause no difficulty.\n\n### 5.1 A fixed vertex\n\nFix a vertex v. Let its normal cone be\nN_D(v)={w: w dot(g-v)<=0 for every g in D}.\nThe full fixed-vertex class is exactly\n\n  x_0=(S+1)v+w, w in N_D(v).                       (5.1)\n\nNecessity follows by summing the constant gradient v and using the terminal\nvertex guard. Sufficiency holds because every prefix has\nx_i=(1+S-s_i)v+w, where s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, and v belongs to N_D(v).\nThus the residual w+(S-s_i)v stays in that cone. All the guards hold.\n\nWrite t=v dot w>=0 and w=t v+w_perp, with w_perp perpendicular to v.\nThe nonnegative sign follows by testing g=0 in the normal inequality.\nThe actual terminal is v+w and\n\n  E_m(x_0)=(1+2t)/[(S+1+t)^2+||w_perp||^2]\n          <=1/D_m.                                (5.2)\n\nIndeed (S+1+t)^2-D_m(1+2t)=(t-S)^2. Equality is attained at\nw=S v, which is legal, giving x_0=D_m v. No transverse coordinate was\ndropped from the denominator in (5.2).\n\n### 5.2 A fixed edge\n\nFix its orthonormal n,t as in Section1 and write x_0=z_0 n+u_0 t.\nThe full fixed-edge class is exactly\n\n  z_0>=(S+1)/2, |u_0|<=b.                          (5.3)\n\nNecessity follows from the initial and terminal edge guards and the normal\nupdate. For sufficiency each physical prefix is\n\n  z_i=z_0-s_i/2>=1/2,\n  u_i=(-1)^i p_i u_0,\n\nwhose tangential guards follow from p_0=1 and (4.2). Thus these are the\nactual updates and the terminal remains on the same edge region. By (3.2),\n\n  E_m(x_0)=[z_0-D_m/4+p^2 u_0^2]/[z_0^2+u_0^2]\n          <=1/D_m.                                (5.4)\n\nThe normal inequality is z_0^2-D_m(z_0-D_m/4)=(z_0-D_m/2)^2>=0;\nthe tangential inequality is (4.6). Equality holds at u_0=0,z_0=D_m/2,\nwhich satisfies (5.3). Therefore\n\n  V_triangle(m)>=1/D_m.                            (5.5)\n\nThese exact fixed-face optima do not bound histories that change faces.\nIn particular (5.5), combined with accepted931's B_n/D_m<47/50, is NOT\nan upper bound on B_n V_triangle(m).\n\n## 6. A legal-prefix obstruction to the radial envelope shortcut\n\nA tempting one-step ingredient for propagating the inscribed-ball radius\nwould be\n\n  ||x-h P_D(x)||<=max{h-1, ||x||-h/2}, h>=1.        (FALSE)\n\nIt is false for every h>2, including an actual bridge of the fixed word.\nUse one edge frame n,t and choose its endpoint v=n/2+b t. Put\n\n  x=2h n+b t, r=||x||=sqrt(4h^2+3/4).\n\nThe edge/vertex boundary guard is exact, so P_D(x)=v. Its next point is\n(2h-h/2)n+(1-h)b t. Direct symbolic expansion gives\n\n  ||x-hv||^2-(r-h/2)^2\n     =h(r-2h)+(3/4)h(h-2)>0.                       (6.1)\n\nAlso r-h/2>=3h/2>h-1, so the right side of the proposed envelope is\nr-h/2. This proves the failure, with an explicit transverse component.\n\nTo realize this at an actual original query, use the level2 bridge\nh=1+a c^2=1+beta c. Here beta>3/4 and c>12/5, so h>2. This is physical\nquery3, preceded by H_2. Initialize at\n\n  x_0=x+S_2 v.\n\nThe residual x-v=(2h-1/2)n is in N_D(v), and adding any nonnegative\nmultiple of v preserves that cone. Thus every preceding gradient is v,\nand summing those actual steps lands at x at query3. The same static F\nthen defines every remaining original point and row by Section2; no\nclaim about its later payoff is made in this counterexample.\n\nThis refutes the radial-envelope proof ingredient, not V_triangle=1/D_m\nand not a full-function upper bound. The second method below retains the\nactual coordinates and does not use this failed envelope.\n\n## 7. Compactness and an explicit finite guard list for every history\n\nFirst reduce the unrestricted initial radius without losing an optimizer.\nSince ||P_D(x)||<=1, every actual run obeys\n\n  ||x_n||<=||x_0||+S, F(x_n)<=||x_n||,\n  E_m(x_0)<=2(r+S)/r^2, r=||x_0||.                 (7.1)\n\nFor r>=R:=4D_m, the last quantity is strictly less than1/D_m. One way\nto check this is that r^2-2D_m r-2D_m S is increasing for r>=4D_m,\nand at4D_m it equals7D_m^2+D_m>0. Meanwhile the punctured ball of\nradius1/2 is covered by (4.4) and has the strictly smaller value (4.6).\nA point attaining1/D_m exists by (5.2). The unrestricted maximum is\ntherefore attained in the compact annulus1/2<=r<=R.\n\nIt is harmless to use the containing square\n\n  K_R={x: -R<=x_1<=R, -R<=x_2<=R}\n\nfor the finite formula: it contains a global optimizer and every extra\npoint is still a legal original initial point.\n\nLet alpha=(sigma_0,...,sigma_n) range over all7^(n+1) strings of the seven face\nlabels. There is a label at the terminal n. Define literal affine products\nfrom the original h, with empty products equal to I:\n\n  U_i=I-h_i P_(sigma_i),\n  M_0=I, e_0=0,\n  M_i=U_(i-1) ... U_0,\n  e_i=-sum_(s=0)^(i-1) h_s [U_(i-1) ... U_(s+1)] b_(sigma_s).\n                                                        (7.2)\n\nThese are finite written products and sums, not executed recurrences.\nEvery U_i is invertible: its eigenvalues are1 and/or1-h_i, with h_i>1.\nIn particular each M_i is invertible.\n\nWrite the individual guards of Section3 as r_guard dot y<=t_guard. Pull every such\nguard at time i back to the same original input x:\n\n  (M_i^T r_guard) dot x <= t_guard-r_guard dot e_i. (7.3)\n\nAdjoin the four square guards. This gives a fully specified finite list\n(c_l,d_l), l=1,...,L_alpha, with c_l in R^2, d_l real. Each c_l is\nnonzero, since the original normals are nonzero and M_i is invertible.\nLet\n\n  C_alpha={x: c_l dot x<=d_l for every l}.          (7.4)\n\nThis notation describes the proof, not an unevaluated feasibility oracle:\nSections8--9 give explicit points and literal sign tests, so no search for\nan x satisfying (7.4) is left in the final expression.\n\nIf x satisfies (7.3), the actual history equals M_i x+e_i by induction:\nthe selected projection is the actual one, and its update is\nU_i y-h_i b_(sigma_i).\nConversely every actual history has such a label at every time and hence\nbelongs to some C_alpha. Shared boundary histories may have several\nlabels, but the actual points and objective agree.\n\nIf sigma_0=C, then x belongs to D, all actual points are in D by Section4,\nand the payoff is p^2. All these strings may be skipped. If sigma_0!=C,\nthe initial edge or vertex guard gives ||x||>=1/2; thus C_alpha stays\naway from0, even when it is lower dimensional. Every C_alpha is compact\nbecause the square guards are included.\n\n## 8. Explicit vertex and edge candidates; no continuous maximization\n\nFor one string with sigma_0!=C abbreviate\nM=M_n,d=e_n,P=P_(sigma_n),b_0=b_(sigma_n).\nBy (3.2) the actual numerator on C_alpha is the quadratic\n\n  q_alpha(x)=x^T A x+2ell dot x+k,\n  A=M^T P M,\n  ell=M^T(Pd+b_0),\n  k=d^T P d+2b_0 dot d-||b_0||^2.                 (8.1)\n\nThe exact payoff is q_alpha(x)/||x||^2. Define a sign filter on any\nexplicit point z: retain it precisely when c_l dot z<=d_l for every\nl and ||z||^2>0. These are finitely many specified real-number comparisons.\n\nDefine Root2(A_0,B_0,C_0) as follows. If A_0!=0 and\nDelta=B_0^2-4A_0C_0>=0, list(-B_0+sqrt(Delta))/(2A_0) and\n(-B_0-sqrt(Delta))/(2A_0). If A_0=0,B_0!=0, list -C_0/B_0.\nOtherwise list nothing. Repeated roots may be kept once. The identically\nzero polynomial is handled in the proofs below and contributes no roots.\n\n### 8.1 Vertices\n\nFor every pair l<r whose row normals are linearly independent, solve the\ntwo linear equations by the displayed inverse\n\n  z= [c_l^T; c_r^T]^(-1) [d_l; d_r].               (8.2)\n\nRetain it if it passes the sign filter. These candidates include every\nvertex of a nonempty compact polygon C_alpha, including a singleton.\nMatrix inversion in (8.2) means the explicit two-by-two adjugate divided\nby its nonzero determinant; it is not a solver invocation.\n\n### 8.2 Stationary points on each guard line\n\nFor each guard c dot x=d_0 put\n\n  C_0=||c||^2>0, u=d_0^2/C_0,\n  z_0=(d_0/C_0)c, v=Jc.\n\nThe line is x=z_0+t v, z_0 dot v=0, ||v||^2=C_0. Put\n\n  a_e=v^T A v,\n  b_e=v dot(Az_0+ell),\n  c_e=z_0^T A z_0+2ell dot z_0+k.\n\nIts ratio is\n\n  (a_e t^2+2b_e t+c_e)/(u+C_0 t^2).\n\nFor every root returned by\n\n  Root2(-b_e C_0, a_e u-c_e C_0, b_e u),            (8.3)\n\nform z=z_0+t v and apply the sign filter. This list includes every\nnonconstant stationary point in the relative interior of a legal edge.\nIndeed differentiating the ratio gives twice the polynomial\n\n  -b_e C_0 t^2+(a_e u-c_e C_0)t+b_e u\n\ndivided by the positive squared denominator. If the guard line passes\nthrough0, a generated point at0 is discarded; C_alpha itself cannot\ncontain0. If this polynomial vanishes identically, the ratio is constant\non the connected feasible segment, and its endpoints have the same value\nand are already covered by (8.2). Compactness supplies those endpoints.\nThis also handles a one-dimensional C_alpha.\n\n## 9. Interior candidates and the singular case\n\nAt an interior stationary point x!=0 let z=q_alpha(x)/||x||^2 be its\nvalue. Differentiating in both coordinates gives\n\n  (A-zI)x=-ell,\n  ell dot x+k=0.                                  (9.1)\n\nIf det(A-zI)!=0, these equations give the explicit candidate\n\n  x=-(A-zI)^(-1)ell                               (9.2)\n\nand the polynomial equation\n\n  Pi(z):=k det(A-zI)-ell^T adj(A-zI)ell=0.         (9.3)\n\nThis has degree at most two. Its coefficients can be written without a\nformal determinant symbol. If\nA=[A_11 A_12; A_12 A_22] and ell=(ell_1,ell_2), then\n\n  Pi(z)= k z^2\n       +[-k(A_11+A_22)+ell_1^2+ell_2^2] z\n       +k(A_11 A_22-A_12^2)\n        -A_22 ell_1^2+2A_12 ell_1ell_2-A_11 ell_2^2.  (9.4)\n\nUse Root2 on these three coefficients. For each listed real z with\nnonzero det(A-zI), form (9.2) and retain it under the sign filter. Equations\n(9.1) then hold, and the ratio at this point is exactly z.\n\nIf Pi is identically zero, its quadratic coefficient gives k=0 and its\nlinear coefficient then gives ||ell||^2=0. Thus (9.2) would only give0,\nwhich is excluded. No nonsingular interior stationary candidate is missed\nby letting Root2 return no roots in this case.\n\nIt remains to justify discarding singular interior stationary values.\nSuppose x is interior, (9.1) holds, and A-zI is singular. Pick a nonzero\nw in its kernel. Symmetry and (A-zI)x=-ell imply ell dot w=0. For every\nreal t, a direct expansion gives\n\n  q_alpha(x+t w)-z||x+t w||^2=0.                  (9.5)\n\nFor example the coefficient of t^2 vanishes by the kernel condition,\nthe linear coefficient vanishes by (9.1), and the constant term is zero\nby the definition of z. Intersect the line x+R w with the compact polygon.\nIt contains a nontrivial segment about x and has boundary endpoints.\nThe polygon is separated from0, so the ratios there are defined and are\nalso z by (9.5). Those endpoints are covered by the boundary reasoning\nin Section8. If A=zI and ell=k=0, the ratio is constant everywhere,\nand any polygon vertex suffices. This proves that no singular stationary\nfamily requires an additional feasibility problem or free parameter.\n\n## 10. The exact finite full-profile expression\n\nLet Z_alpha be the finite list of all points retained from (8.2), (8.3),\nand (9.2). It is generated solely by the literal products (7.2),\nnonzero determinant divisions, Root2, and the explicit sign filter.\nEmpty lists contribute no value. Define the finite set of real numbers\n\n  L_m={p^2,1/D_m}\n      union over all alpha with sigma_0!=C\n             {q_alpha(z)/||z||^2: z in Z_alpha}.     (10.1)\n\nThen the exact whole-initial-state identity is\n\n  V_triangle(m)=max L_m.                            (10.2)\n\nProof of the upper direction: a global maximum exists in the square by\nSection7. If its initial label is C, its value is p^2. Otherwise it is a\nmaximum of the continuous ratio over some C_alpha. If that polygon is a\npoint or a line, Section8 supplies a candidate of the same value. In two\ndimensions, a boundary maximum is a vertex, a nonconstant edge stationary\npoint, or on a constant edge, all covered by Section8. An interior maximum\nsatisfies (9.1); either (9.2) supplies it or (9.5) moves the same value to\nthe boundary. Thus its value occurs in L_m.\n\nProof of the lower direction: every sign-filtered candidate belongs to a\nlegal full original history by Section7 and is evaluated with its own\nactual Euclidean radius and the actual terminal polynomial. The extra\nvalues p^2 and1/D_m are attained by Sections4--5. Thus no value in L_m\nexceeds V_triangle(m). This proves (10.2).\n\nNo convex hull of one nonlinear image is propagated through another map;\nno incompatible history is selected for a child. Every guard for the\nentire string is imposed on the SAME explicit point z. In particular,\nreal support reentries and coordinate-order reversals are included.\n\nFor fixed m all entries before Root2 belong to\nK=Q(sqrt(2),sqrt(3)), a field of degree4 over Q: m and epsilon are rational,\nall h_i,D_m are in Q(sqrt(2)), and the body coordinates and guard normals\nare in K. Vertices are in K^2. An edge candidate is in K(sqrt(Delta))^2\nfor one Delta in K. An interior candidate is in K(z)^2 with z satisfying\none quadratic over K. Its payoff belongs to that same field. The maximum\nof a finite set is one of its members. Therefore\n\n  [Q(V_triangle(m)):Q]<=8.                          (10.3)\n\nThis degree statement concerns the value selected by the exact finite\nlist; it does not claim that the exponentially many sign tests have been\nsimplified, or that the maximum is known to be a particular listed value.\n\n## 11. What remains unresolved, and why the finite formula is not a certificate\n\nThe outstanding comparison is fully concrete:\n\n  For all m>=1536, is every retained value in(10.1) <=1/D_m?\n  If not, is there an explicit uniform legal family with a nonvanishing gain?\n\nThe answer is not supplied by the algebraic degree bound. It requires a\nuniform argument controlling all legal face-changing strings, or a\nparticular all-depth legal selector and its quantitative payoff. Merely\nsaying that one can evaluate the finite expression would not be a proof\nof either assertion. No such evaluation is authorized or performed here.\n\nTwo direct strategies were considered within mechanism1. A radius envelope\nbased only on the inscribed ball is refuted by Section6. Mechanism2 removes\nall continuous optimization from the full domain, but does not turn the\nremaining exponentially indexed comparisons into an all-m estimate.\n\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 was read in full. It proves the unconditional\noriginal all-integer-split benchmark inequality B_n/D_m<47/50 here, and\nonly conditionally derives a negative minimax answer if the FULL W obeys\n17/(16D_m). It is the only accepted comparison invoked in this discussion.\nEven a proof that THIS triangle has V_triangle<=17/(16D_m) would concern\none fixed function family and would not prove that full-W hypothesis.\nConversely a triangle gain over1/D_m would only be a lower witness for\nthis particular H_m; it would not prove the original every-word lower bound.\n\nThe original problem retains all finite dimensions, attained minima,\nevery sufficiently large original horizon, every nonnegative predetermined\nword, and the exact all-integer-split benchmark. None of those quantifiers\nis replaced by the present fixed-body diagnostic.\n\n## 12. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is used only at its stated benchmark\nand hierarchy scope. No external mathematical theorem is imported.\n\n## 1. Original model, same hierarchy, and symbolic bounds\n\nFor an arbitrary nonnegative word h of length n, normalize to smoothness\none and a specified minimizer x_star=0, f_star=0. The original iterates\nand interpolation residuals are\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nEvery legal transcript has Q_ij<=0 for all ordered distinct pairs,\nincluding the optimum. The objective is W=2R: a certificate at level\nchi must bound 2f_n/||x_0||^2 in every finite dimension. In a full\nquadratic certificate all original formal gradients remain independent.\n\nFor the diagnostic family fix any integer m>=1536 ONCE and put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,\n    R=n-1, K=n-2, I={1,...,K}.\n\nThus the requested interface has singleton first sector0, all\nindependent middle gradients indexed by I, and singleton last queried\nsectorR. No physical query or last leaf is removed. Let\n\n    A=h_0=h_R=1+a,\n    J=2^(m-1)-1,\n    t=a*c^(m-1), T=h_J=1+t,\n    s=sum H_(m-1).\n\nHere J is the actual root-step index, S=2s+T, and the suffix strictly\nafter J has mass s. Throughout this report T is the root step, whereas\nt is that step minus one. These scalar symbols are local to this\nreport; they do not replace any project benchmark capacity.\n\nThe following loose bounds suffice, and require no numerical\nevaluation:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2.\n\nFor the first bounds, sqrt(2)>7/5 gives lambda>12/5, while\nsqrt(2)<10/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 give\nc<(17/7)(6145/6144)<5/2. Also beta>99/100, so\na>(99/100)/(5/2)=99/250>1/3. Finally\na<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.\n\nFor completeness the exact hierarchy mass can be obtained symbolically.\nWriting S_j=sum H_j and v=a/(c-2), literal concatenation gives\n\n    1+S_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j.\n\nThe formula has the required initial value and satisfies\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j because v(c-2)=a. Moreover\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, so 0<v<1. Consequently,\n\n    S_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    S/T<c/a<15/2,\n    t>c^(m-1)/3>17, T>18.\n\nAll steps exceed one, so S>n and D>2^(m+1)-1. In particular,\n\n    chi_target=17/(16D)<2^(-m).\n\nThe final strict inequality follows from\n2^(m+1)-1>(17/16)2^m for m>=1. This establishes every parameter\ncomparison used below at every allowed m without executing a recurrence.\n\n## 2. Mechanism1: an explicit original chronological transport\n\nChoose the following literal formulas:\n\n    b_i=h_i/S                    (0<=i<=R),\n    F_i=(sum_(t=0)^i h_t)/S      (0<=i<R).\n\nUse the half-multiplier convention lambda_ij=2mu_ij, and set\n\n    mu_(i,star)=b_i              (0<=i<=R),\n    mu_(i+1,i)=F_i               (0<=i<R),\n    mu_(n,R)=1.\n\nEvery other multiplier is zero. In particular there are actual\nmiddle-to-middle rows Q_(i+1,i) for 1<=i<K. This is not a fan through\none algebraic middle mean. The coefficients depend on the physical\nchronological masses, including the actual long dyadic steps.\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative. They are finite sums with a positive\ndenominator, not a recursively evaluated or implicitly defined law.\nTheir signs therefore close at every depth of the stated hierarchy.\n\nThe value balance is separate at every original node. At0 it is\nb_0-F_0=0. At every 1<=i<R it is\n\n    b_i+F_(i-1)-F_i=0.\n\nAt R, outgoing mass is b_R+F_(R-1)=1 and incoming terminal mass is1.\nAt n, the outgoing mass is1. Thus sum mu_ij Q_ij has value coefficient\none at f_n and zero at every other finite f_i. The optimum value is\nfixed to zero. No equality between intermediate sampled values is used.\n\nDefine the one global quadratic\n\n    C_chi=chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu_ij Q_ij.\n\nOnly this one initial/common charge is present. We now expand the\nentire polynomial, rather than testing just its collapsed sector.\n\n## 3. Entire transport remainder and the actual free-terminal square\n\nPut\n\n    g_b=sum_(i=0)^R b_i g_i,\n    w=A+1/2-A^2/2.\n\nDirectly from the actual final update x_n=x_R-A g_R,\n\n    Q_(n,R)=f_n-f_R+(A-A^2/2)||g_R||^2\n                         +||g_n+(A-1)g_R||^2/2.\n\nThis identity retains the free formal terminal g_n. Expanding the\nother original rows gives the exact full-space identity\n\n    C_chi=chi||x_0||^2-2<g_b,x_0>+2K_ch\n                              +||g_n+(A-1)g_R||^2,          (3.1)\n\nwhere\n\n    K_ch=sum_(i=0)^(R-1) F_i||g_i||^2+w||g_R||^2\n       +sum_(0<=i<j<=R) h_i*b_j <g_i,g_j>\n       +sum_(i=0)^(R-1) F_i(h_i-1)<g_i,g_(i+1)>.          (3.2)\n\nHere is an independent coefficient check. The optimum rows are\n\n    Q_(i,star)=f_i-<g_i,x_0>\n                  +sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nThe transport rows are\n\n    Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+(h_i-1)<g_(i+1),g_i>\n                             +(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2.\n\nFor i<R the combined half-diagonal is\n(b_i+F_(i-1)+F_i)/2=F_i, with F_(-1)=0. At R it is\n(b_R+F_(R-1))/2+A-A^2/2=w. The optimum-row chronological\ncross terms and the adjacent-row cross terms are precisely the two\nsums in (3.2). This accounts for every term and both middle endpoints.\n\nNo indefinite storage is suppressed: K_ch is the full remainder,\nand its sign is still to be tested. In particular, positive transport\ndiagonals do not by themselves prove it positive semidefinite.\n\n### Exact relationship with the former signed variance problem\n\nLet P_i=sum_(l=1)^(i-1)h_l be the middle mass before i, let\nB=sum_(i in I)h_i, and let d_I=sum_(i in I)h_i g_i. Set the actual\nouter formal vectors x_0=g_0=g_R=g_n=0 only to display the middle\nprincipal form of (3.2). Since b_i=h_i/S, it is exactly\n\n    K_I=||d_I||^2/(2S)\n        +sum_(i=1)^K d_i||g_i||^2\n        +sum_(i=1)^(K-1) e_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>,                 (3.3)\n\n    d_i=[A+P_i+h_i(2-h_i)/2]/S,\n    e_i=F_i(h_i-1).\n\nIndeed the all-pairs chronological sum in (3.2) is\n[||d_I||^2-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2]/(2S). Equation (3.3) shows\nexactly the attempted payment: the signed h_i(2-h_i)/(2S)\ndiagonal is supplemented by the positive cumulative-mass term\n(A+P_i)/S, but this payment introduces the full adjacent bilinear\nload e_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>. Static legality and exact original interpolation rows\n\nThe vertices satisfy ||v_i||=1, sum_i v_i=0, <v_i,v_j>=-1/2 for i!=j, and\nsum_i v_i v_i^T=(3/2)I. Thus\n\n  D={x:<x,v_i>>=-1/2 for all i},\n  ball(0,1/2) subset D subset ball(0,1).\n\nCompleting the square in F identifies its unique maximizer as g=P_D(x).\nThe defining necessary and sufficient projection inequality is\n\n  <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment toward\nz; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. This derives (3.2)--(3.3) without an external theorem.\n\nThe actual objective is\n\n  Psi_C=Q(u,v),\n  Psi_V=2u+v-1,\n  Psi_E=(3u^2+2u+4v-1)/4. Define a single\nstatic derivative and its objective by\n\n    g(x)=min(kappa_plus*x,b_plus) for x>=0,\n    g(x)=max(kappa_minus*x,-b_minus) for x<=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.\n\nSet\n\n    K=max(kappa_plus,kappa_minus),\n    q=min(kappa_plus,kappa_minus),\n    q_cut=1/(1+d_(m-2)).\n\nWrite Psi_j(s) for the actual GD output from s under the full word H_j\non this fixed f, using the physical steps, not a curvature-rescaled word.\n\nThe principal new complete candidate is\n\n    q<=q_cut  =>  sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n                           =K/(1+2*K*S). In particular it includes\nthe previously uncovered region\n\n    0<q<=q_cut<K<=1. Every history that reverses from that ray back to the ray of\ncurvature K satisfies the strict bound\n\n    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2\n       < (2250/2401)*K/(1+2*K*S). Static legality, true distance, and every interpolation row\n\nFor sigma in{plus,minus}, define the physical radius threshold\nu_sigma=b_sigma/kappa_sigma. On that ray, with r=|x|,\n\n    f(x)=kappa_sigma*r^2/2,                    r<=u_sigma,\n    f(x)=b_sigma*r-b_sigma^2/(2*kappa_sigma),  r>=u_sigma.\n\nThe formulas agree in value and derivative at the threshold. The\nderivative g is continuous, nondecreasing, and piecewise affine with\nslopes in[0,1]. Partitioning any interval at its finitely many breakpoints\ntherefore gives |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. The\ntrue initial distance for a nonzero start s is exactly |s|.\n\nFor an arbitrary advertised L>0, take F=L*f and use steps h_i/L.\nThe physical iterates are unchanged, and\n2*(F(x_n)-F(0))/(L*s^2)=2*f(x_n)/s^2. The construction therefore has\nthe required original normalization and is admissible for every L.\n\nAt every physical index, including the terminal, set\n\n    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),\n    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n_m.\n\nFor x<y, let Delta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. On[x,y],\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).\n\nIntegration bounds both corresponding remainders below by Delta^2/2.\nThese are the two ordered inequalities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0. This proves every remote, revisited,\nthreshold, minimizer and terminal row of the original scalar data.\nThe full Gram matrix is positive semidefinite because its quadratic\nforms are squares of real linear combinations of the scalar vectors.\nThe terminal remains the actual, unaltered output of the last update.\n\n## 3. The upper bound a<1/2 follows\nfrom beta<1 and c>2.\n\nPut eta=a/(c-2). Since (1+sqrt(2))*((1+sqrt(2))-2)=1,\nc*(c-2)>1>beta. Hence0<eta<1. Summing the literal tree gives\n\n    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j. In particular\n\n    d_j/A_j<c-2<3/7<1/2 for all j>=0,\n    A_j<c^j for j>=1. Thus their masses P,T satisfy\n\n    P>=S_k, T>=S_k,\n    P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                               (3.4)\n\nNo choice of physical occurrence is excluded by this statement.\n\nWe will also need a root-to-child mass ratio. Put j=m-1. Since j>=5,\nd_j>(2/5)*2^5=64/5>9. From (3.3), A_j>7*d_j/3, giving\n\n    S_j>7*d_j/3-1>2+2*d_j=2*h_j^lev.\n\nTherefore, for every K>0,\n\n    (1+2*K*S_m)/(1+2*K*S_j)<5/2, j=m-1.             (3.5)\n\nHere S_m=2*S_j+h_j^lev<(5/2)*S_j; the constant term in the\ndenominator only makes the displayed ratio smaller. This estimate is\nuniform in K, even when K depends on m or tends to zero.\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: exact signed coordinate and objective-energy transport\n\nFor each ray define\n\n    y_sigma=sqrt(kappa_sigma)*r,\n    B_sigma=b_sigma/sqrt(kappa_sigma),\n    J_B(y)=y^2/2 for0<=y<=B,\n           B*y-B^2/2 for y>=B.\n\nThen f(x)=J_(B_sigma)(y_sigma) exactly. Write E_sigma=2*J_(B_sigma)\n(y_sigma) for twice the objective on a ray. Its inverse radius map is\n\n    chi_B(E)=sqrt(E),                  0<=E<=B^2,\n             (E+B^2)/(2*B),           E>=B^2.         (4.1)\n\nThus y is a curvature-weighted physical radius, not the square root of\ntwice the objective on a saturated tail. This distinction is retained.\n\nFor a physical step h>=0 set t_sigma=h*kappa_sigma. For an individual\ninput on ray sigma at coordinate y, form\n\n    z=y-t_sigma*min(y,B_sigma).\n\nIf z>=0 the output stays on that ray with coordinate z. If z<0 it\ncrosses to the other ray tau with coordinate\n\n    sqrt(kappa_tau/kappa_sigma)*(-z).               (4.2)\n\nIn particular both effective step sizes and both thresholds remain\nsign dependent. On consecutive crossings the square-root curvature\nratios multiply to sqrt(kappa_final/kappa_initial), but the factors\nt_sigma-1 and every clipping test remain. Their telescoping is not a\ncommon-curvature reduction.\n\nFor a ray input interval0<=y<=Y define its exact same-ray and cross-ray\noutput maxima by\n\n    Stay_sigma(Y)=[Y-t_sigma*min(Y,B_sigma)]_+,\n    Cross_(sigma->tau)(Y)\n       =sqrt(kappa_tau/kappa_sigma)*(t_sigma-1)_+\n                                      *min(Y,B_sigma). (4.3)\n\nHere[z]_+=max(z,0). To prove (4.3), the scalar map before a crossing\nhas slope1-t_sigma in the core and slope1 in the tail. If its core\nslope is nonnegative, it is increasing and never crosses. If its core\nslope is negative, the largest opposite excursion is at the smaller\nof Y and B_sigma. Its same-ray positive maximum is at the outer\nendpoint or at0. These observations also prove the formulas at\nt_sigma=1, t_sigma=2 and Y=B_sigma, without deleting any boundary.\n\nThe signed coordinate x -> sign(x)*sqrt(kappa_sign(x))*|x| is a\ncontinuous increasing bijection. For an interval containing0 whose\nsigned-coordinate radii are(Y_minus,Y_plus), the exact next radii are\n\n    Y_minus'=max(Stay_minus(Y_minus),Cross_(plus->minus)(Y_plus)),\n    Y_plus' =max(Stay_plus(Y_plus), Cross_(minus->plus)(Y_minus)). (4.4)\n\nThe image is an interval containing0 because the physical update is\ncontinuous and fixes0. All extrema in (4.4) are attained. The map is\ncoordinatewise nondecreasing as a map of interval bounds, even when\nthe point update is not monotone.\n\nEquivalently, let T_h be the exact map of twice-energy endpoint caps.\nIts component on ray sigma is\n\n    (T_h(E_minus,E_plus))_sigma\n      =2*J_(B_sigma)(max(\n           Stay_sigma(chi_(B_sigma)(E_sigma)),\n           Cross_(tau->sigma)(chi_(B_tau)(E_tau)))). (4.5)\n\nDefine T_[H_j] by chronological composition of these maps through the\nliteral word H_j. This is exact for the whole interval, not a choice\nof incompatible child maximizers: at every step the entire previous\ninterval is the actual continuous image of the original interval.\nBoth energy coordinates and their fixed thresholds are retained.\n\nFor starts |s|<=R, the initial caps are\n\n    E_sigma,0=2*J_(B_sigma)(sqrt(kappa_sigma)*R).\n\nThe sought full-family bound is equivalent to both coordinates of\nT_[H_m](E_(minus,0),E_(plus,0)) being at most K*R^2/(1+2*K*S).\nFor necessity apply a pointwise bound to the actual start attaining an\nendpoint, whose radius is at most R; for sufficiency choose R=|s|.\nThis exact interface is a reformulation, not a solved upper bound.\n\n## 5. The attempted joint geometric energy invariant is false\n\nThe concrete attempted extension is a common-K geometric plateau for\nthe two true energy endpoints, with both thresholds kept. Set\n\n    theta=a*beta, w=min(B_minus,B_plus), R_j=K*d_j.\n\nFor the levels where a common-K plateau would be used, the attempted\ninvariant was, for EVERY integer r>=0,\n\n    T_[H_j](2*J_(B_minus)(R_j*w*theta^r),\n            2*J_(B_plus) (R_j*w*theta^r))\n       <=(w^2*theta^(2*r+2),w^2*theta^(2*r+2)),      (5.1)\n\ncomponentwise. This is a two-energy, two-threshold assertion, not a\nradius assertion with the destination energy replaced by K*x^2.\nIf it held at j=m, every positive start with y_plus=1 and1<=R_m*w\nwould, by selecting its geometric cell, have terminal twice-energy\nless than1/R_m^2. The following SAME complete static witness refutes\nthat implication, hence refutes (5.1).\n\nRetain exactly the previous witness67baf005c4c5eed4:\n\n    kappa_plus=1, kappa_minus=mu=1/(4*S),\n    b_plus=1, b_minus=a/(8*S), x_0=1,\n    u_plus=1, u_minus=a/2.\n\nIts first physical step ell gives x_1=-a. For1<=i<=n_m define the\nactual post-first-leaf prefix mass\n\n    T_i=sum_(t=1)^(i-1) h_t.\n\nEvery subsequent physical state, including the terminal, is exactly\n\n    x_i=-a+b_minus*T_i.                            (5.2)\n\nIndeed0<=T_i<=S-ell<S, so its radius is greater than7*a/8>a/2.\nThus every subsequent gradient is the same negative saturated\ngradient-b_minus of this one function, proving every update in (5.2).\nThe complete minimum set is{0}, its own initial radius is1, and all\nordered rows follow from Section2.\n\nThe terminal radius and twice-objective are\n\n    r_term=a*(7/8+ell/(8*S)),\n    E_term=2*f(x_(n_m))=a^2*(5*S+ell)/(32*S^2).      (5.3)\n\nIts large radius remains the original counterexample to the max-K\nphysical radial bound: d_m>20, a>2/5 imply d_m*a/2>1 and\nr_term>7*a/8>1/d_m. Thus its initial radius1 is in the advertised\nold physical plateau range, while its output violates that bound.\nIts energy also refutes the new plateau:\nusing S<c^m and a>2/5 from Section3,\n\n    E_term*d_m^2\n       >5*a^4*c^m/32>c^m/250>1.                   (5.4)\n\nThe last comparison follows from c>2 and m>=1536>=8, so c^m>256.\nHere K=1, B_plus=1, B_minus=a/(4*sqrt(S))<1, and w=B_minus.\nFurthermore\n\n    R_m*w=a^2*c^m/(4*sqrt(S))\n           >sqrt(c^m)/25>1.                       (5.5)\n\nThe last inequality follows from m>=10 and sqrt(c^m)>2^(m/2)>=32.\nSince0<theta<1, there is an integer r>=0 with\n\n    R_m*w*theta^(r+1)<1<=R_m*w*theta^r.\n\nIf1 is a geometric boundary, choose it as the upper endpoint of its\ncell. The input x_0=1 is included in the corresponding interval for\n(5.1), so that invariant would give\nE_term<=w^2*theta^(2*r+2)<1/d_m^2, contradicting (5.4).\n\nThis is a full-word physical failure of the energy plateau. It does\nnot refute an objective profile of order1/S. In fact S>=ell>1 and\na<1/2 give\n\n    E_term<=3*a^2/(16*S), D_m<3*S,\n    D_m*E_term<9/64<1.                             (5.6)\n\nThe same function, same thresholds, same actual negative clipped\nsuffix and same terminal have been preserved. No new improving\nobjective witness is claimed. Merely changing from radius to energy\ndoes not preserve the common-curvature d_m^(-2) plateau scale. This\ndecisive failure activates the second and final mechanism.\n\n## 6. Mechanism2: exact monotone ray segments and a single crossing\n\nThe estimates in this section are proved jointly and used below; they\nare not imported from the earlier unverified common-curvature report.\n\nConsider any segment staying on one ray of positive curvature p and\nthreshold u, with physical mass T. Let its initial and terminal radii\nbe r and z. Radii are nonincreasing along such a segment because every\nupdate is inward and, by assumption, does not cross. Every queried\ngradient magnitude is at least p*min(z,u). Thus\n\n    r>=z+p*T*min(z,u).                              (6.1)\n\nIf z<=u, twice the terminal objective is p*z^2, and (6.1) gives\nthe bound p*r^2/(1+p*T)^2<=p*r^2/(1+2*p*T).\nIf z>=u, twice the objective is p*(2*u*z-u^2); the identity\n\n    (z+p*T*u)^2-(1+2*p*T)*(2*u*z-u^2)\n       =(z-(1+p*T)*u)^2>=0                         (6.2)\n\ngives the same upper bound. The tie z=u is included in both formulas.\nHence every such segment satisfies\n\n    2*f(terminal)<=p*r^2/(1+2*p*T).                 (6.3)\n\nWhen all steps in a block satisfy h*p<=1, its ray radius map is exactly\nthe chronological composition of\n\n    r -> r-h*p*min(r,u), r>=0.                     (6.4)\n\nEach map is nonnegative, nondecreasing, piecewise affine with slopes\n1-h*p and1, and at most its input. Thus this is a genuinely monotone\none-side block, including h*p=1. Inequality (6.3) controls its true\nterminal energy whether the terminal is quadratic or clipped.\n\nNow consider a history with exactly one strict sign crossing. Let\nits source and destination curvatures be p and v, its crossing step\nbe h, and its physical prefix and suffix masses be P,T. The entire\nprefix is on the source ray. For pre-crossing radius y, write\nw=min(y,u_source), so the query gradient magnitude is p*w.\nIf the initial radius is R, monotonicity of the prefix gives\n\n    R>=y+p*P*w>=(1+p*P)*w.\n\nThe crossing requires h*p>1 and has landing radius\n\n    z=h*p*w-y<=(h*p-1)*w\n                    <=[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]*R.        (6.5)\n\nIts suffix stays on the destination ray. Combining (6.3) and (6.5),\n\n    2*f(terminal)/R^2\n       <=[v/(1+2*v*T)]*[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]^2.        (6.6)\n\nThese source-to-destination factors use the true two curvatures.\nThe derivatives of the positive factors with respect to their own\ncurvatures are\n\n    d/dv [v/(1+2*v*T)]=1/(1+2*v*T)^2,\n    d/dp [(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]=(h+P)/(1+p*P)^2.\n\nThey are positive. Thus if both curvatures are at most K, (6.6) is\nat most its expression with both parameters replaced by K. This is\nmonotonicity of a proved scalar upper expression; it asserts no\ncurvature monotonicity of actual GD trajectories.\n\n## 7. Every single crossing at every physical position of H_j\n\nFor any0<K<=1 and any level-k occurrence in H_j, a crossing with\nsource curvature at most K is bounded using the preceding section.\nLet\n\n    C=1+K*S_k, e=K*h_k^lev-1.\n\nIf e<=0 no curvature at most K crosses there. Otherwise e>0 and\n\n    e/C<=d_k/A_k<c-2<1/2.                          (7.1)\n\nFor the first inequality, the ratio\n(K*h_k^lev-1)/(1+K*S_k) increases in K and is d_k/A_k at K=1.\n\nSet N=K*(S_j-S_(k+1))>=0 and x=K*(P-S_k). By (3.4),0<=x<=N and\n\n    (1+K*P)^2*(1+2*K*T)\n       =(C+x)^2*(2*C-1+2*N-2*x).                  (7.2)\n\nThe derivative of the right side with respect to x is\n2*(C+x)*(C-1+2*N-3*x). Its sign can change only from positive to\nnegative. Therefore its minimum on[0,N] is at an endpoint. The\nvalue at N minus the value at0 is\n\n    N*(2*C*(C-1)+N*(2*C-1))>=0.\n\nConsequently (7.2) is at least C^2*(2*C-1+2*N). On the other hand,\n\n    1+2*K*S_j=4*C+2*e-1+2*N.\n\nUsing e/C<1/2 and C>=1,\n\n    e^2*(4*C+2*e-1)/(C^2*(2*C-1))\n       <(5*C-1)/(4*(2*C-1))<=1.                   (7.3)\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to3*(C-1)>=0. Adding the2*N\nterms preserves the required inequality because e^2<C^2. It follows\nthat\n\n    e^2/[(1+K*P)^2*(1+2*K*T)]\n          <=1/(1+2*K*S_j).                         (7.4)\n\nTogether with (6.6), this proves the following complete conditional\nhistory bound: on ANY H_j with the fixed enclosing-m parameters, a\nhistory having at most one strict sign crossing satisfies\n\n    2*f(Psi_j(s))/s^2<=K/(1+2*K*S_j).               (7.5)\n\nA no-crossing history follows from (6.3) and monotonicity of\np/(1+2*p*S_j) in p. A hit of zero remains zero forever and has\nzero terminal gap, so it does not create a missing crossing case.\nFor j=0, (7.5) is the immediate 2*f(s)<=K*s^2. This includes both\nleaves at the ends of every nonempty word and every possible cut.\n\nOne further radius estimate will pay for the root return. A FIRST\ncrossing in H_j, at level k, has source curvature p<=1 and physical\nprefix P>=S_k. By (6.5), its landing radius z satisfies\n\n    z/|s|<=(p*h_k^lev-1)/(1+p*P)\n       <=d_k/(1+P)<=d_k/A_k<c-2.                  (7.6)\n\nThe middle comparison again follows by increasing p to1 in this\npositive upper expression. This is a first-crossing bound, not a\nclaim of radial contraction under an arbitrary returning suffix.\n\n## 8. Split at the first level where both signs can reverse\n\nReflect the fixed function if necessary so that the ray of curvature\nq is negative and the ray of curvature K is positive. Reflection also\nexchanges the two fixed clipping levels and all starts, and leaves the\nprofile unchanged. If the curvatures tie, either labeling is allowed.\n\nDefine the first common reversing level\n\n    J_q=min{j in{0,...,m-1}: q*h_j^lev>1},\n\nand set J_q=infinity if this set is empty. Since K>=q, the same level\nalso permits a core reversal on the K-ray. The strict inequality is\nthe correct crossing condition: at equality a core query lands at0,\nand a tail query stays on its original ray or lands at0.\n\nAssume q<=q_cut. Put j=m-1. Every physical step in each of the two\nH_j children obeys q*h<=q*h_(m-2)^lev<=1. Thus the negative ray is\ninvariant throughout either child, with the exact monotone maps (6.4).\nA child started positive may stay positive or cross once to negative;\nafter that it cannot return. Every child history has at most one sign\ncrossing. Equation (7.5) consequently proves its ALL-START bound\n\n    2*f(Psi_j(z))<=K*z^2/(1+2*K*S_j) for every z.   (8.1)\n\nThe first common reversing level is either J_q=m-1 or infinity.\nIn the infinity case the full word itself has at most one crossing,\nso (7.5) proves its required bound immediately.\n\nIn the root case the complete physical word is exactly\n\n    H_j, h_j^lev, H_j.\n\nThere is no later ancestor beyond the root. The right H_j in this\ndisplay is the ENTIRE actual suffix, including its final leaf ell.\nWe now retain its true objective through (8.1), instead of stopping\nat the post-root radius.\n\n## 9. The root return, including a preceding and a following crossing\n\nIf no negative-to-positive crossing occurs, a nonzero full history\nhas at most one positive-to-negative crossing. Equation (7.5) on H_m\nthen gives the sharp upper bound. Zero hits have zero gap as before.\n\nOtherwise the sole negative-to-positive crossing is the root. Let\nR=|s|, let y>0 be its pre-root negative radius, and let z>0 be its\npost-root positive radius. Put\n\n    e_q=q*h_j^lev-1>0, j=m-1.\n\nBecause q<=1/h_(j-1)^lev and d_j=c*d_(j-1),\n\n    e_q<=h_j^lev/h_(j-1)^lev-1<c-1.                (9.1)\n\nThe strict step-ratio comparison follows from\n1+c*d_(j-1)<c*(1+d_(j-1)), since c>1. The exact root formula gives\n\n    z=q*h_j^lev*min(y,u_minus)-y<=e_q*y.             (9.2)\n\nThere are two exhaustive initial-sign cases.\n\nIf s<0, the whole first child stays negative. Applying the prefix\nargument in (6.5) directly through that child gives\n\n    z/R<=e_q/(1+q*S_j)<=d_j/A_j<c-2.               (9.3)\n\nThe second comparison increases q to1 in the positive ratio; it is\nnot a replacement of either true trajectory curvature.\n\nIf s>0, the first child ends negative, so it has exactly one\npositive-to-negative crossing. Equation (7.6) bounds its landing\nradius by(c-2)*R. Every remaining update of that first child is on\nthe invariant negative ray and is nonincreasing in radius by (6.4).\nTherefore y<(c-2)*R. Equations (9.1)-(9.2) give\n\n    z/R<(c-1)*(c-2)<(10/7)*(3/7)=30/49.            (9.4)\n\nThis bound holds for every source clipping and destination clipping\nchoice, including saturated root queries. A sharper optional bound\nretaining the exact first cut is also available: if its prefix and\npost-cut child masses are P,T and its level is k, applying (6.5)\nto the first cut and then to the monotone negative segment through\nthe root gives\n\n    z/R<=[(K*h_k^lev-1)/(1+K*P)]\n                    *[e_q/(1+q*T)].                (9.5)\n\nThis expression is not needed for (9.4), but shows explicitly where\nthe true low-curvature gradient drainage could sharpen the transfer.\nIt imposes no clipping reset or independent choice of a child start.\n\nSince c-1>1, (9.4) also bounds the low-start case (9.3). The actual\nright child starts at this actual positive z; it may remain positive\nor cross once more to negative. Its full objective is bounded by\n(8.1). Exact attainment and final new domain theorem\n\nThe no-return bound and (9.6) cover every start under q<=q_cut. For\nattainment let the K-ray threshold be u_high=b_high/K, and set\n\n    D_K=1+2*K*S_m, s=D_K*u_high\n\non that ray.
